### LOG-0027 · 2026-09-30 · One iOS umbrella framework from a `:core:ios` export module (DEC-058)

- **Event:** `CONF-40` was resolved by owner decision: the iOS app links exactly one Kotlin framework, produced by a new Kotlin Multiplatform module `:core:ios` that declares the two Apple targets of ADR-0002 and one `binaries.framework` exporting the five `:feature:*` modules and the four other `:core:*` modules through `api`. ADR-0001's module set is amended by one module; ADR-0003's sharing boundary and hand-written binding are untouched; a new backlog row `TASK-078` (M2) carries the work, and `TASK-051` now depends on it.
- **Rationale:** `CONF-40` recorded that no module could both reach the feature-owned state types the iOS app consumes and stay inside ADR-0001's dependency direction. Per-feature frameworks — the alternative — are the arrangement the Kotlin Multiplatform documentation itself calls limited (*"Usage of several Kotlin/Native frameworks in a Swift application is limited, but you can create an umbrella framework and export all these modules to it"*, `multiplatform-build-native-binaries.html`, verified 2026-09-30) and would each package `kotlin-stdlib` and `kotlinx-serialization`, duplicating the runtime against `REQ-NFR-002` and risking duplicate-symbol linkage. The umbrella module also keeps the decision reversible in one build file: no feature module references the framework, so the recorded fallback is a change to `:core:ios` and the Xcode linkage only.
- **Affected artifacts:** new `docs/adr/0012-ios-framework-export.md`; `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md` (`DEC-058`, §5); `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`CONF-40` moved to §6.1, §6.3 row updated, §9); `docs/adr/0001-module-boundaries.md` (amendment pointer and the two diagram edges); `docs/DESIGN.md` (§3 diagram, §3.1 table, §3.4 rule 10, §3.5, §10); `docs/BACKLOG.md` (`TASK-078` added, `TASK-051` dependency, §2.4, §8.2, §9); `README.md` §5 and §8, `README.es.md` §5 and §8, `docs/HANDOFF.md` §3 and §8.
- **Decision / ADR:** DEC-058 (new), amending DEC-052 / ADR-0001; relates to ADR-0002, ADR-0003 and ADR-0008's containment pattern. `DEF-004` is the expiry condition.
- **Validation:** Documentation change only: no code was executed for it, and no framework has been produced. The decision's claims are cited from the Kotlin Multiplatform binary documentation (retrieved 2026-09-30) and from the repository's own ADR-0001 and ADR-0003. The export topology's behaviour with `@Serializable data object` route declarations and `sealed interface` intent hierarchies is **not** verified and is the first thing `TASK-078`'s spike must observe; an ADR-0012 whose exported surface proves unusable is superseded rather than patched. Internal links in the edited documents were re-checked, and the two modified Mermaid diagrams were checked for undeclared nodes.
### LOG-0028 · 2026-09-30 · Publication authorised, commit identity pinned to the owner account

- **Event:** The repository owner instructed that the repository be published and that every commit from now on be authored and committed as `David Ruiz <davidru85@gmail.com>`. The identity is pinned **repository-locally** (`file:.git/config`), not globally, so it applies to this project only. The owner also asked for a project-log note, which is this entry; the publication itself follows this commit.
- **Rationale:** `DEC-045` already fixes a **public** repository with a public read-only Figma link and committed PNG exports, so the private state of the repository contradicted an accepted, Blocking decision. Publication also removes the reviewer's need for a GitHub account, which is the same accessibility problem `CON-005` records for the Figma file. On identity: an agent working in this repository inherits the git configuration, so without a pinned identity a commit could carry an unintended author — that is a defect in the record, not a cosmetic issue, and it is now prevented at the repository level.
- **Affected artifacts:** the git configuration's `user.name`/`user.email` for this repository; the repository's visibility and About metadata (external to the tree); this log. No document content changes.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-045` (public repository) becomes true rather than aspirational; the commit-identity instruction is an owner directive of 2026-09-30 with no `DEC-###` of its own yet, because it is a repository setting rather than a project decision. Whether it becomes a standing rule — and therefore needs a `DEC-###` row and a line in `CONTRIBUTING.md` §3 — is recorded below as an open point for the owner.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-09-30, before publishing. The identity is confirmed in place: `git config --show-origin --get user.email` → `file:.git/config` → `davidru85@gmail.com`, and the same for `user.name` → `David Ruiz`. A secret scan was re-run over the **entire history** and the working tree — 159 text blobs, 12 credential patterns (private keys, AWS, GitHub, Slack, Google, Stripe, JWT, keystores, credential assignments, basic-auth URLs, `.env` assignments) — and found **0 matches**. The author/committer inventory of all branches was taken and **published with LOG-0029**, because it is a fact about the published artifact.
- **Not verified:** the publication itself, and the About metadata, are not claimed here; that is `LOG-0029`. Whether `DEC-045`'s Figma-access clause (`CON-005`) is resolved is not claimed either: publishing the repository does not grant access to the Figma file.
- **Consequence the owner accepted explicitly:** the history of the repository **will contain `davidru85@gmail.com` in 30 author positions and 26 committer positions** — 18 pre-audit commits, the three `docs/documentation-system` commits authored as `davidru85@users.noreply.github.com`, and every commit of TASK-014 and after. The four merge commits `8026eb2`, `6b8f028`, `f48dea5` and `9c3193a` carry `noreply@github.com` as committer, which is GitHub's own bot. The owner was told that publishing makes that address harvestable and still authorised publication; the alternative — rewriting history with a mailmap — is a prohibited action for an agent (`AGENTS.md` §4.2) and was not performed.
### LOG-0029 · 2026-09-30 · Repository published, About metadata completed, branch protection available but not configured

- **Event:** The repository was switched from private to public and its About metadata was completed: description, homepage `https://rickandmortyapi.com/`, and thirteen topics. Anonymous access to the repository and to both `README.md` and `README.es.md` was then confirmed without credentials. Branch protection became available as a consequence of publication and was **deliberately not configured**, because there is no CI to satisfy required checks and a required-check rule with no workflow would deadlock every pull request. The default branch remains `main` with no protection and no ruleset.
- **Rationale:** `DEC-045` fixes a public repository, so the private state contradicted an accepted, Blocking decision; publication also removes the reviewer's need for a GitHub account, the same class of accessibility problem `CON-005` records for the Figma file. Required checks were withheld because `TESTING.md` §14.2's check names are provisional, no workflow exists (`GAP-002`, TASK-025), and marking a non-existent check required leaves a pull request permanently "Expected — waiting for status to be reported", which is exactly the deadlock `DEFINITION.md` §7 warns against.
- **Affected artifacts:** the repository's visibility, its About metadata and the availability of branch protection and rulesets (all external to the tree); this log. No document in the tree describes the repository as private, so no documentation change follows from the visibility switch itself.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-045` is now true rather than aspirational. `CON-005` is **not** resolved: publishing the repository does not grant access to the Figma file, which still returns 403 to anonymous clients, and `docs/figma/` exports remain pending (`TASK-035`).
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-09-30, after publication. `gh api repos/davidru85/RickAndMorty` → `visibility: public`, `default_branch: main`, `license: none`. The same call returns description, homepage and thirteen topics as set. Anonymous probes with no credentials: `GET api.github.com/repos/davidru85/RickAndMorty` → **HTTP 200**; `raw.githubusercontent.com/.../README.md` → **HTTP 200**; `raw.githubusercontent.com/.../README.es.md` → **HTTP 200**, which confirms the language variant is served publicly. `GET branches/main/protection` fails with *"Upgrade to GitHub Pro or make this repository public"* before publication and, after it, the endpoint is reachable, which is how the availability change was observed.
- **Not verified:** nothing about the applications, the build or the test suites, which are unchanged by this entry — the state recorded in LOG-0026 stands. No CI run exists, and no required check has ever reported on this repository.
- **Consequences observed on publication, none of which block the review:**
  - **Anyone can fork, clone and index the full history.** The `davidru85@gmail.com` exposure recorded in LOG-0028 is now realised, not hypothetical.
  - **`docs/PROJECT_LOG.md` is public**, including `LOG-0016`'s note about `API_SPECS.md` §9 still carrying pre-audit rate-limit wording. That inconsistency is public now, so correcting it in `API_SPECS.md` §9 has a higher value than it did while the repository was private.
  - **Community health files do not show.** GitHub presents `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md` and `SUPPORT` only from the repository root, `.github/`, or `docs/`. `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` and `docs/SECURITY.md` exist, but GitHub looks for `CONTRIBUTING.md` and `SECURITY.md` by that exact name, so the community-profile checklist still reports them missing. Surfacing them is a repository-layout decision, not a metadata field, and is left to the owner.
  - **`license: none`** is reported by GitHub. No `LICENSE` file exists and none was invented; the choice between no licence, MIT and Apache-2.0 is the owner's, and `assessment.md` contains material authored at ZARA, which is a factor in that choice.
  - **No branch protection and no ruleset** exist, so `DEC-054`'s mandatory check set is a convention rather than a gate, exactly as `CONTRIBUTING.md` §5.3 and `DEFINITION.md` §7 state. `GAP-002` remains open and unchanged.
### LOG-0030 · 2026-09-30 · `main` protected by a ruleset, without required checks or approvals

- **Event:** The owner instructed that `main` be protected, having been told both the policy position and the deadlock risk. A **repository ruleset** (`main protection`, id `24241444`, enforcement `active`) now applies to `refs/heads/main` with four rules: `deletion`, `non_fast_forward`, `required_linear_history` and `pull_request` (`required_approving_review_count: 0`, `required_review_thread_resolution: true`, `allowed_merge_methods: ["rebase", "merge"]`). The owner holds a permanent bypass (`actor_type: User`, `actor_id: 472324`, `bypass_mode: always`). The classic branch-protection API was not used because the ruleset API is GitHub's current replacement for it.
- **Rationale:** `required_linear_history` plus `allowed_merge_methods` of rebase and merge is the mechanical enforcement of `DEC-053`/`DEC-041`: squash merges are prohibited, so the TDD phase commits survive integration. Two rules are deliberately **absent**: required status checks, because no workflow exists (`GAP-002`, TASK-025) and a check that never reports leaves a pull request permanently "Expected — waiting for status to be reported"; and required approvals, because the repository has one collaborator (`davidru85`), GitHub does not permit self-approval, and one required approval would make every pull request unmergeable. The permanent bypass exists so the sole maintainer can always land a fix on `main`.
- **Affected artifacts:** the repository's ruleset (external to the tree); `docs/HANDOFF.md` §6, `docs/TECHNICAL_PLAN.md` §2.1, `docs/GUIDELINES.md` §8 and `docs/TESTING.md` §14.2, each of which stated that no protection existed, plus this log.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-054` is now **partly** enforced: the pull-request requirement and the linear-history requirement are real, the mandatory check set is not. `DEC-049` is unaffected — repository settings remain human actions, and this one was instructed by the owner. `TASK-028` owns the remaining half (required checks bound to branch protection), and `GAP-002` stays open.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-09-30 from the API, not from the UI. `GET repos/davidru85/RickAndMorty/rulesets/24241444` → `enforcement: active`, rules `deletion`, `non_fast_forward`, `required_linear_history`, `pull_request`, `allowed_merge_methods: ["rebase","merge"]`, `required_approving_review_count: 0`, bypass `User:472324 always`. `GET repos/davidru85/RickAndMorty/rules/branches/main` → the four rule types, which confirms the ruleset is **effective** on the branch rather than merely stored. `GET repos/.../commits/<sha>` for `ea525df`, `6e507ca`, `d673a0a`, `2c7b4b9`, `fc6f15b` and `dd694f9` → every one reports `author.login = davidru85`, so the ruleset's `require_extra_approval_for_unattributed_changes` default does not create a second approval requirement. PR #6 reports `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, `mergeStateStatus: CLEAN`, which is the evidence that the configuration did not lock the pending review.
- **Not verified:** that a direct push to `main` is refused, and that a squash merge is refused, were **not** executed: attempting either would write to the default branch or to a pull request's history, and `git push --dry-run` does not evaluate server-side rulesets, so it cannot serve as evidence. The ruleset's effectiveness is therefore evidenced by the rules endpoint and by the four named rules, not by a failed write. No check has ever reported on this repository and none is required.
### LOG-0031 · 2026-09-30 · Integration switched to merge commits; rebase merging disabled (DEC-059)

- **Event:** The owner instructed that every pull request be merged into `main` **without** a rebase merge, so that the previous branch and its commits are always preserved as a record. Executed on the repository: the `main` ruleset's `pull_request` rule now allows `allowed_merge_methods: ["merge"]` only, the `required_linear_history` rule was removed from it, and the repository-level merge settings became `allow_merge_commit: true`, `allow_rebase_merge: false`, `allow_squash_merge: false`, with `delete_branch_on_merge: false` confirmed rather than changed. The ruleset keeps `deletion`, `non_fast_forward` and `pull_request`, its `active` enforcement, its zero required approvals and its owner bypass.
- **Rationale:** Owner directive of 2026-09-30. A rebase merge rewrites the branch's commits with new SHAs and the source branch is then deleted, so the pre-merge branch as a named, fetchable reference stops existing — which is exactly the record the owner wants kept. `required_linear_history` is what GitHub uses to refuse merge commits, so it had to be removed for the instruction to be satisfiable; leaving it in place would have made the setting inert. Squash merging remains disabled, because it destroys the red/green/refactor commit sequence DEC-053 rests on.
- **Affected artifacts:** the `main` ruleset (id `24241444`) and the repository's merge settings (both external to the tree); `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md` (`DEC-059`, `DEC-041` marked superseded in its merge-method half, the §3 superseded table, §5); `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` §3.5, `docs/TECHNICAL_PLAN.md` §8.2 and `docs/GUIDELINES.md` §7.5, each of which mandated a linear history and a rebase merge and was false after the change; this log.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-059` (new, Accepted) supersedes the **merge-method half** of `DEC-041` and supersedes the rebase-merge row added to §3. `DEC-053`'s phase-commit protocol is untouched: a merge commit preserves those commits in the merge's second parent, whereas a rebase merge rewrote them. Trunk-based development and Conventional Commits, the other half of DEC-041, are unchanged. No ADR is required: this is a process decision, not architecture (`DECISION_BOARD.md` §1).
- **Trade-off accepted knowingly:** history on `main` is no longer linear. `main` gains one merge commit per pull request, which is the cost of keeping the branch record. The previous configuration had chosen the opposite trade-off.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-09-30 from the API after the change. `GET repos/.../rulesets/24241444` → `enforcement: active`, rules `deletion`, `non_fast_forward`, `pull_request` (**`required_linear_history` absent**), `allowed_merge_methods: ["merge"]`, bypass `User:472324`. `GET repos/.../rules/branches/main` → the same three rule types, confirming the ruleset is effective on the branch rather than merely stored. `GET repos/davidru85/RickAndMorty` → `allow_merge_commit: true`, `allow_rebase_merge: false`, `allow_squash_merge: false`, `delete_branch_on_merge: false`. Links and placeholders were re-checked across the touched documents.
- **Not verified:** that the GitHub UI now offers only "Create a merge commit" and that a rebase merge is refused were **not** executed, because both would require opening a pull request and merging it, and the branch record would be the thing at risk. The evidence is the configuration read back from the API, plus the fact that `required_linear_history` is the rule GitHub uses to reject merge commits.
- **Consequence for the next pull request:** the first change merged after this entry will produce a merge commit on `main`, so `git log --first-parent main` becomes the reviewable list of integrated changes while plain `git log main` still shows the branch commits. That is the intended shape, and the earlier note in `LOG-0030` that the protection existed partly to enforce linear history no longer applies.
- **Observed limitation this decision cannot repair retroactively:** the owner also asked for the branch history to remain readable from the graph. That holds for the merges done *after* this entry, and it already holds for #1–#4, which are real merge commits (`8026eb2`, `6b8f028`, `f48dea5`, `9c3193a`). It does **not** hold for merges **#6 and #7**: both were rebase merges, performed while `required_linear_history` was still in force and before the setting changed, so `main` has no branch structure across them and their branch tips (`044767d`, `51c7ab0`) are unreachable from `main`'s graph; `51c7ab0` survives only in the reflog of the machine that deleted it. Making that retroactive would require rewriting `main`'s history, which is a prohibited action for an agent (`AGENTS.md` §4.2) and is not proposed. The mitigation is that the **names** are not lost: every historical branch remains listed by its pull request (`gh pr list --state all`), which is where the branch name lives permanently even after the ref is deleted.
- **Verified by a controlled experiment, not by assertion:** in a throwaway clone, a branch with two commits was merged with `--no-ff` and then deleted with `git branch -D`. Afterwards `git log --graph --oneline --all` still showed both of its commits on a side line converging at the merge commit, `git branch --contains <branch-tip>` reported `main`, and `git log --merges` still printed the branch name in the merge subject. That is the property DEC-059 buys, and it is the property a rebase merge does not have.
### LOG-0032 · 2026-09-30 · The version catalog pins the planned inventory and the dependency policy becomes a build check (TASK-015)

- **Event:** The version catalog became the single, exact, justified and auditable source of every external version the project plans to use. `gradle/libs.versions.toml` now pins the closed inventory of TASK-015: the versions an accepted decision fixes, verbatim, and a selected-and-verified version for every entry no decision fixes. `docs/DESIGN.md` §3.5 gained a machine-readable rationale table (one row per catalog entry, with its effective version, concern, solution, a cited identifier, a primary-source URL and a verification date) plus a "Not pinned" register. `README.md` §15 and `README.es.md` §15 gained the dependency inventory: every entry with its version and its **real declaration state**, derived from the build scripts. A new convention plugin, `multiverse.dependency.policy`, applied by the root build script only, registers `verifyDependencyPins` (`TEST-UNIT-014`), `verifyDependencyRationale` (`TEST-UNIT-013`) and `verifyDependencyInventory` (`TEST-UNIT-051`) and wires `verifyDependencyPolicy` into the root `check`. `docs/TESTING.md` allocates `TEST-UNIT-051`. No module build script changed: every new entry is pinned ahead of use (DEC-060) and is declared by the task that first uses it.
- **Rationale:** `assessment.md` l.7 — "use them wisely, each third party library added is a dependency" — is the reason the task exists, and `REQ-NFR-002` and `REQ-NFR-006` are the requirements it serves. Documentation alone would leave the claim unenforceable: a policy that is only remembered decays, which is why each rule is a build check proven against a seeded violation. Pinning the full planned inventory ahead of first use is the owner's OD-2 directive (DEC-060), and enforcing it with Gradle verification tasks rather than a unit-test source set is OD-3 (DEC-061).
- **Affected artifacts:** `gradle/libs.versions.toml`; `build-logic/convention/build.gradle.kts`; `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/` (nine new files); the root `build.gradle.kts`; `docs/DESIGN.md` §3.5 and §10; `README.md` §9, §11, §14 and the new §15; `README.es.md` in mirror; `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md` (`DEC-060`, `DEC-061`, §5); `docs/TESTING.md` (preamble, §3.3, §13.1, §14.2, §16, §17, §18); `docs/GUIDELINES.md` (§1.4, §3.6); `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` §5.3 and §7; `docs/SECURITY.md` §9.3; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`CONF-45`…`CONF-51`, `GAP-010`, §5, §7, §8, §9); `AGENTS.md` §1.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-060` and `DEC-061` (new, Accepted); `DEC-010`, `DEC-011`, `DEC-014`, `DEC-026`, `DEC-032`, `DEC-034`; ADR-0004, ADR-0006, ADR-0007, ADR-0008. No new ADR: both decisions are tooling decisions (`DECISION_BOARD.md` §1).
- **Validation:** Executed on 2026-09-30 from branch `build/version-catalog-inventory`, JDK 25 daemon, Android SDK platform 37. Every pin was verified against a primary source with an HTTP probe: Maven Central, Google Maven and the Gradle Plugin Portal all answered `200` for each decided and each selected entry. Observed: `./gradlew help --warning-mode=all` → success, so every generated accessor compiles; `./gradlew verifyDependencyPolicy` → the three tasks execute and pass, first run "Configuration cache entry stored", second run "reused"; `./gradlew build` → BUILD SUCCESSFUL including the new tasks; `./gradlew check` → runs `verifyDependencyPolicy`; `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:validatePlugins` → success with no warning; `:androidApp:assembleDebug` with no `iosApp/` present → success. The seeded-violation matrix S1–S12 was executed in full: each row was observed to fail the **specific** rule that owns it (`1.11.+`, `latest.release`, `[1.0,2.0)`, an inline coordinate, a missing wrapper checksum, an ungoverned versionless entry, a catalog entry with no rationale row, an empty Rationale cell, a third solution for one concern, a row version differing from the catalog, a deleted inventory row, a Pinned entry marked Declared, and a Spanish-only version change), and each passed again once reverted, with `git diff --exit-code` confirming a clean tree. Not verified: no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so D2 (required checks green on both runners) cannot be met; compile-level compatibility of every Pinned entry is proven when its task first declares it, not here; the iOS app does not exist.
- **Consequences recorded, not resolved:** the unowned obligation of `SECURITY.md` §9.3 becomes `GAP-010` (Gradle dependency-verification metadata or a lockfile; recommended as a dedicated task after TASK-025, because platform-specific artifacts need checksums generated on every CI OS). `CONF-45` (one or two solutions per concern) becomes explicit. `CONF-46` (whether the documentation-phase write limit still applies) stays open. `CONF-47` (`:core:domain` and `Flow`) is pinned around, coroutines being required by `IC-008`. `CONF-48` (test-id mapping) and `CONF-50` (no first-party `material-color-utilities` artifact) are recorded, as is `CONF-51` (the detekt stable line documents no Kotlin 2.4.20 combination). `CONF-44` becomes partially resolved, and `CONF-49` records the stale TASK-014 and `.gitignore` claims this change corrects.

### LOG-0033 · 2026-09-30 · The TASK-015 review round: fourteen defects corrected, the policy checks hardened

- **Event:** The review of PR #10 found fourteen defects in TASK-015, and all fourteen were corrected on the same branch and pull request, with new commits only. **One specification deviation:** `io.gitlab.arturbosch.detekt` 1.23.8 was pinned although no rule of TASK-015 spec §6.3.C admits it — its own rationale said the vendor documents no Kotlin 2.4.20 / Gradle 9.7 / AGP 9.3.1 support, and the 2.x line is alpha-only in every published version — so the pin was removed under OD-6 and the entry moved to the "Not pinned" register with `CONF-51` as its blocked-item record. **Eight policy-check defects, each reproduced on the unmodified head before being fixed:** a `prefer`-only or `reject`-only constraint counted as versionless and bypassed P1; `governingBom` allowed a versionless BOM to govern itself and DEC-060's single BOM was quoted but not enforced; P4 missed five of seven inline-version forms (chained `.version("…")`, named arguments, concatenation, `kotlin("module","version")`, the constraint DSL); a comment or a string literal mentioning an accessor counted as a declaration (a false inventory state); bundles and catalog name lookups were invisible to the reference scan; P4 reported a bare file name although twelve scanned scripts share one; the rationale check skipped the two toolchain rows; and three defects were reported two or three times each. **Four documentation defects:** the `GUIDELINES.md` §1.4 claim that every rule was seeded was not yet true; `DESIGN.md` §10 had no TASK-015 row; two lists carried wrong numbers; and dead code and a stale KDoc note survived. **One PR-metadata defect:** issue #9 was not linked for closing.
- **Rationale:** The policy checks are the point of TASK-015: they exist so that `REQ-NFR-002` and `REQ-NFR-006` are mechanical rather than remembered, and four of the defects let a violation pass unnoticed, which defeats the requirement rather than merely inconveniencing it. detekt is the clearest case of the rule working as written: TASK-015 spec §6.3.C admits a pin only when the vendor documents support within this project's toolchain, and detekt does not, so OD-6 applies instead of a convenient approximation.
- **Corrects LOG-0032:** detekt is no longer pinned, so the entry count is 47 and `Pinned` is 39; the enforced rule set is P1–P6 (pins), R1–R7 (rationale) and I1–I8 (inventory), not the P1–P5/R1–R6/I1–I6 of LOG-0032; the seed matrix is S1–S34, and each seed was observed to fail the owning rule and to pass once reverted; and `DESIGN.md` §10 now carries the row LOG-0032 announced. Everything else in LOG-0032 stands.
- **Affected artifacts:** `gradle/libs.versions.toml`; `docs/DESIGN.md` §3.5 and §10; `README.md` and `README.es.md` §9 and §15; the nine original files plus `KotlinSourceMask.kt`, `Locations.kt` and `PropertiesFiles.kt` under `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/`; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`CONF-51`, §7, §9); `docs/GUIDELINES.md` §1.4 and §3.6; `docs/HANDOFF.md` §1.1 and §4; `docs/TESTING.md` §18; this log.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-057`, `DEC-060`, `DEC-061`, ADR-0008; TASK-015 OD-6. No new `DEC-###`, `TEST-###` or `TASK-###` id was needed: every added rule belongs to `TEST-UNIT-013`, `TEST-UNIT-014` or `TEST-UNIT-051`.
- **Validation:** Executed on 2026-09-30 on branch `build/version-catalog-inventory`, JDK 25 daemon, Android SDK platform 37. `./gradlew verifyDependencyPolicy` passes and stores then reuses the configuration cache; `./gradlew build` succeeds with the three tasks executed; `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:validatePlugins` succeeds with no warning; `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds with no `iosApp/` present; a clean clone of the pushed head builds. The full S1–S34 matrix was run, with the defects reproduced on `e8b753f` first: S13, S14, S16, S17, S18, S19, S20, S21, S23, S24, S25, S26, S29a and S29b each passed the check they should have failed before the fix, and each failed with one precise line after it. `./gradlew buildEnvironment` is identical at the fix's parent and at its head, so the plugin classpath did not change.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so D2 cannot be met and no required check has ever reported on this repository; the compile-level compatibility of every `Pinned` entry is proven when the task that owns it first declares it, not here; the iOS app does not exist. The redesign of P4 and I5 on Gradle's dependency model — declared `ExternalModuleDependency` constraints and `pluginManagement.resolutionStrategy.eachPlugin` rather than source-text scanning — is recorded as a follow-up proposal in the pull request with no task id, because it is out of scope for this round.

### LOG-0034 · 2026-09-30 · Second TASK-015 review round: the checks stop failing open and stop scanning the wrong files

- **Event:** The second review of PR #10 found that the policy checks still failed open in several shapes and, separately, scanned the directory tree rather than the build. Twelve defects (D-01…D-12) were corrected on the same branch and pull request with new commits only. **The checks failed open:** a `prefer`-only or `reject`-only constraint counted as versionless and bypassed P1; a versionless BOM governed itself and DEC-060's single BOM was unenforced; P4's digit guard let through exactly the dynamic values `AC-REQ-NFR-006-1` forbids (`version = "+"`, `strictly("[1.0,2.0)")`, `id("x").version("latest.release")`) and matched line by line, so a form split across lines passed; a comment or a string literal mentioning an accessor counted as a declaration; a chain written across lines, an aliased catalog and a precompiled script plugin were invisible to I5; a second version catalog escaped all three tasks; and R7 checked a toolchain row only if it existed. **The checks scanned the wrong files:** the file set came from `fileTree(rootDir) { include("**/*.gradle.kts") }` with `.gradle/**` and `.kotlin/**` excluded only at the root, so a nested checkout inside the repository, and `build-logic/.gradle/` or `build-logic/.kotlin/`, were scanned as projects; Groovy scripts and `buildSrc` were never scanned at all. **Reporting and records:** the inventory task stopped at the first missing README table; one location used a bare file name; an unknown accessor was reported twice; the `/*` and raw-string mask lexing was wrong; and LOG-0033 misstated the S23/S24 evidence.
- **Rationale:** A check that fails open defeats `REQ-NFR-006` and `REQ-NFR-002` — it certifies exactly the state it exists to forbid — and a check that fails on a nested checkout blocks `check` for a reason unrelated to the change under review, which trains the team to ignore it. The source-text approach has now needed two rounds of patterns, so this round closes the practical holes, states the remaining limits, and tracks the Gradle-model redesign as `GAP-011` so the limits have an owner rather than a PR footnote.
- **Corrects LOG-0033:** S23 and S24 are **negative** seeds: before the fix they wrongly **failed** and after it they **pass**, not the other way round. The phrase "one defect yields exactly one line" becomes "one line per `[versions]` alias and per catalog entry", because a defective shared value is reported for the alias and for each entry that uses it. Everything else in LOG-0033 stands.
- **Affected artifacts:** the 15 files under `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/` (three new: `KotlinSourceMask.kt` and `Locations.kt` from round 1, `GradleWrapper.kt` here); `docs/DESIGN.md` §3.5; `docs/GUIDELINES.md` §1.4 and §3.6; `docs/TESTING.md` §18; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`GAP-011`, §8, §9); `docs/HANDOFF.md` §1.1 and §4; this log.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-057`, `DEC-060`, `DEC-061`; `GAP-011`. No new `DEC-###`, `TEST-###` or `TASK-###` id was needed: P7, P8 and I9 belong to `TEST-UNIT-014` and `TEST-UNIT-051`. The rule set is now P1–P8, R1–R7 and I1–I9.
- **Validation:** Executed on 2026-09-30 on branch `build/version-catalog-inventory`, JDK 25 daemon, Android SDK platform 37. `./gradlew verifyDependencyPolicy` passes and stores then reuses the configuration cache; `./gradlew build` succeeds with the three tasks executed; `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:validatePlugins --rerun-tasks` succeeds with no warning; `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds with no `iosApp/` present; a clean clone of the pushed head builds; a nested-checkout seed followed by `./gradlew check` on the final head succeeds. The full **S1–S53** matrix was run, S1–S34 as regressions and S35–S53 new, each row observed to fail the owning rule with one precise line and to pass once reverted, with `git diff --exit-code` clean after every revert. The defects were reproduced on `2563940` first: S35–S41, S43, S44, S48, S49, S51 and S53 wrongly passed, and S46, S47 and S50 wrongly failed. `buildEnvironment` is identical at the round's parent and at its head.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so D2 cannot be met and no required check has ever reported on this repository. The compile-level compatibility of every `Pinned` entry is proven when the task that owns it first declares it. The remaining source-text limits L1–L4 are `GAP-011`. The iOS app does not exist.

### LOG-0035 · 2026-09-30 · Third TASK-015 review round: the checks stop failing open on source forms, both Gradle builds are covered, and the tables are validated

- **Event:** A third review of PR #10 found eight further defects (F-01…F-08) and reproduced eight evidence cases (E-01…E-08) on the then-head `1f5cb33`; all were corrected on the same branch and pull request with new commits only. **Enforcement still failed open:** a rich version written as `{ require = "1.11.0" }` resolved to the same `requiredVersion` as the permitted plain string, so P1 certified a source form TASK-015 §6.2 forbids; P7 read only the main build's `VersionCatalogsExtension`, so a catalog declared in the included `build-logic` build passed; P8 inspected only root `buildSrc` and root `settings.gradle`, so Groovy build logic in the included build and `build-logic/buildSrc` passed; and P6 rejected only a BOM with different coordinates, so two aliases of the Compose BOM both passed. **The inventory used directories for identity:** main project paths were reconstructed from the file-system directory, so a legitimate `projectDir` remap produced a false `Declared by` value. **The Markdown checks assumed the grammar they claimed:** rows were compared as a set rather than in catalog order, and a cell with surrounding prose was accepted as a single code span. **The records carried contradictions:** `HANDOFF.md` stated that the build invoked by the §8 commands does not exist and that no test or linter has executed, `GUIDELINES.md` §1.4 stated that no analyser configuration exists, and LOG-0034 attributed round-1 defects to round 2 and claimed every S1–S53 row fails after the fix although six rows are negative seeds that must pass.
- **Rationale:** The policy checks are the deliverable of TASK-015, and each fail-open case certifies exactly the state `REQ-NFR-006` exists to forbid: a rich version form is not a plain pin, a catalog the checks cannot see is not a single catalog, and a declaration hidden from the scan is not an inventoried dependency. A check that fails on a legitimate `projectDir` remap is the mirror-image defect: it blocks a correct change for a reason unrelated to the dependency policy. The record defects matter for the same reason as the code defects — a document that contradicts the observed build teaches the reader to distrust both.
- **Corrects LOG-0034:** (1) LOG-0034's Event paragraph repeats round-1 defects (`prefer`/`reject` constraints, a self-governing BOM, and comment/string accessor false positives) as if they were round-2 findings; the actual round-2 additions were the D-01…D-12 set of the round-2 prompt, and the round-3 review found the four enforcement gaps above. (2) LOG-0034's Validation sentence is wrong where it says every S1–S53 row failed its owning rule after the fix: S22, S23, S24, S46, S47 and S50 are **negative** seeds and must pass. (3) The round-2 claim that main project paths came from the build model was not true until F-05 in this round; until then `projectPathOf` derived them from directories. (4) The rule set remains P1–P8, R1–R7 and I1–I9 — this round strengthened existing rules and allocated no new number.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/` (new `CatalogSource.kt`; `DependencyPinsTask.kt`, `DependencyPolicyPlugin.kt`, `DependencyInventoryTask.kt`, `DependencyRationaleTask.kt`, `MarkdownTable.kt`, `BuildScripts.kt`); `docs/HANDOFF.md` §1.1 and §7; `docs/GUIDELINES.md` §1.4; `docs/TESTING.md` §18; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`GAP-011`, §7, §9); `docs/DESIGN.md` §3.5; this log.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-057`, `DEC-060`, `DEC-061`; `GAP-011` extended. No new `DEC-###`, `TEST-###` or `TASK-###` id was needed: F-01 and F-04 belong to `TEST-UNIT-014`, F-02 and F-03 extend P7 and P8 under `TEST-UNIT-014`, and F-05 and F-06 belong to `TEST-UNIT-051` and `TEST-UNIT-013`.
- **Validation:** Executed on 2026-09-30 on branch `build/version-catalog-inventory`, JDK 25 daemon, Android SDK platform 37. `./gradlew verifyDependencyPolicy` passes and stores then reuses the configuration cache; `./gradlew build` succeeds with the three tasks executed; `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:validatePlugins --rerun-tasks` succeeds with no warning; `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds with no `iosApp/` present; a clean clone of the pushed head builds; `git diff --check origin/main...HEAD` reports nothing; every relative link in every touched document resolves. The full **S1–S63** matrix was run — S1–S53 as regressions and S54–S63 as this round's seeds — with each positive seed failing its owning rule with one precise line and each negative seed passing, and `git diff --exit-code` clean after every revert. The new defects were reproduced on `1f5cb33` first: E-01 (`{ require = "…" }`), E-02 (a second catalog in `build-logic`), E-03 (`build-logic/buildSrc`), E-04 (`build-logic/seed.gradle`), E-05 (a duplicate Compose BOM alias), E-07 (swapped inventory rows) and E-08 (an Entry cell with prose) all passed; E-06 (a `projectDir` remap) produced a false `Declared by` failure. `./gradlew buildEnvironment` is unchanged. The clean-state source scan confirms: no `*.gradle` file in either build root, no `buildSrc` in either root, no second catalog name, no rich version key in `gradle/libs.versions.toml`, and exactly one BOM library entry.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so D2 cannot be met and no required check has ever reported on this repository; no product test source set, no iOS app and no app run exists; the compile-level compatibility of every `Pinned` catalog entry is proven when the task that owns it first declares it. The source-model limits of P4, P5, I5 and I8 remain `GAP-011`, now naming the supported settings and catalog syntax and the residual cases (L1–L4).

### LOG-0036 · 2026-09-30 · TASK-016: the `.gitignore` is completed and the repository-hygiene check becomes executable

- **Event:** TASK-016 completed on branch `build/repository-hygiene`. The root `.gitignore` kept every JetBrains KMP-template rule and the intentional `prompts/` rule, and gained groups for environment files, Android/Apple signing material, key material, service credentials, editor state (`.vscode/`, `.fleet/`) and Apple build output (`DerivedData/`, `iosApp/.build/`, `*.xcuserstate`). A new dependency-free root convention plugin, `multiverse.repository.hygiene` (`build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/hygiene/`), registers `verifyRepositoryHygiene`, implements `TEST-UNIT-026` and is wired into the root `check` beside the dependency-policy plugin. It holds the path rules `HYG-01`…`HYG-07` (every §6.1 ignore sentinel is ignored by Git's own `check-ignore` semantics, no shared Xcode/project file is over-ignored, no tracked path is ignored or prohibited, no credential-carrier path appears in reachable history, no symlink escape, no submodule), and scans the commit-eligible working set (tracked plus untracked, non-ignored files) and every unique blob reachable from all local refs for twelve credential classes `SEC-026-01`…`SEC-026-12`. It fails closed on a shallow checkout, a non-Git directory, a Git error, an incomplete object set, a submodule or an escaping symlink, and prints findings redacted to rule id, path, line and blob identity. Recorded as `DEC-062`; the tooling TDD exception applies (`DEFINITION.md` §3 D4) and was proved by seeded violations instead.
- **Rationale:** `REQ-SEC-002` and `AC-REQ-SEC-002-1` require no secret in the repository, and a one-time manual scan cannot prevent a future regression. `.gitignore` was already tracked, so the task's remaining gap was an executable check: it makes the ignore rules and the credential rules verifiable on every `check` and `build`, and it states its own boundary — the scan is a fixed-pattern policy, not a proof that no secret can exist.
- **Affected artifacts:** `.gitignore`; `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/hygiene/` (`RepositoryHygienePlugin.kt`, `RepositoryHygieneTask.kt`, `GitRepository.kt`, `HygieneRules.kt`, `HygieneViolation.kt`); `build-logic/convention/build.gradle.kts`; root `build.gradle.kts`; `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md`; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `docs/TESTING.md`; `docs/SECURITY.md`; `docs/GUIDELINES.md`; `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md`; `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md`; `AGENTS.md`; `README.md`; `README.es.md`; this log.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-062` (new tooling decision; no ADR, because the mechanism is a repository check rather than architecture), plus `DEC-035`, `DEC-046`, `DEC-049`, `DEC-053`, `DEC-054`, `DEC-057`. `TEST-UNIT-026` is now implemented; `TASK-016` moves to `In review`; `TASK-048` gains `TASK-016` as a dependency and re-runs the check rather than building a second scanner.
- **Validation:** Executed on 2026-09-30 on branch `build/repository-hygiene`, JDK 25 daemon, Android SDK platform 37. Clean state: `./gradlew verifyRepositoryHygiene` passes with 95 commit-eligible paths, 671 reachable objects, 307 unique blobs scanned, 12 credential rule classes, 38 ignore sentinels and 0 findings; a second run and a configuration-cache reuse both re-execute the task (never `UP-TO-DATE`); `./gradlew check` and `./gradlew build` succeed and include it; `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds with no `iosApp/`; `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:validatePlugins --rerun-tasks` succeeds with no warning; `git ls-files -ci --exclude-standard` is empty; all 38 sentinels are ignored by `git check-ignore -v` and the shared Xcode and project files are not; a clean clone of the branch passes the same commands non-shallowly. The **S1–S14** seed matrix was run in disposable clones: S1 (`HYG-01`), S2 (`HYG-02`+`SEC-026-12`), S3 (`HYG-02`+`HYG-03`), S4 (`SEC-026-04`), S5 (`SEC-026-01`), S6 (`SEC-026-10`+`SEC-026-12`), S7 (`SEC-026-11`), S8 (`SEC-026-02`, `03`, `05`, `06`, `07`, `08`), S9 (history-only `SEC-026-04` with a clean tip), S10 (committed carrier, `HYG-04`+`SEC-026-09`), S11 (`HYG-06` on a `--depth 1` clone), S12 (`HYG-07` on an escaping symlink), S13 and S14 clean passes; every positive seed was removed and the clean state passed again, and no seed value appeared in any captured output. No seed was committed to the branch; history seeds ran only in disposable clones.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so D2 cannot be met and no required check has ever reported on this repository, and branch protection does not yet require the future `repository-hygiene` check; the scan covers the fixed registry and the path classes only and is not a guarantee against every possible secret; history means the blobs reachable from fetched local refs, not dangling objects or refs that were never fetched; no product test, app run, iOS build or performance measurement was performed.

### LOG-0037 · 2026-09-30 · TASK-016 review corrections: the hygiene scan stops failing open

- **Event:** An independent review of PR #13 at head `e38019a` proved that `verifyRepositoryHygiene` could report `0 findings` while a credential-shaped blob was directly reachable from a local ref (F-01), while a forbidden historical carrier path was reachable but not named by `git rev-list --objects` (F-02), and while `git cat-file --batch` exited non-zero before scanning (F-03); that it missed a common quoted JSON credential assignment (F-04); that a tracked `.env` or key container produced no `HYG-03` (F-05); and that the PR carried no GitHub-recognised `Closes #12` (F-06). All six were corrected on `build/repository-hygiene` with new commits only, and the existing PR #13 was updated. No decision changed: `DEC-062` states the intended contract and the implementation now satisfies it.
- **Rationale:** `REQ-SEC-002` and `AC-REQ-SEC-002-1` are only met by a check that cannot certify an unscanned object. Each false negative certified exactly the state the check exists to forbid — a reachable historical credential, a re-used carrier path, or an unread content stream reported as a clean scan — so the corrections close the gap rather than weaken the rule.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/hygiene/` (`GitRepository.kt`, `HygieneRules.kt`, `RepositoryHygieneTask.kt`); `docs/PROJECT_LOG.md`; `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `docs/TESTING.md`; `docs/SECURITY.md`; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md`; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `README.md`; `README.es.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-062` (contract unchanged), `DEC-035`, `DEC-046`, `DEC-049`, `DEC-053`, `DEC-054`, `DEC-057`. No new `DEC-###` — a correction to an implementation, not a decision change. `TASK-016` stays `In review`.
- **Corrections.** F-01: the content scan set is every reachable object Git classifies as a `blob`, so a blob a local ref points to directly with no tree path is scanned; the reported count is the number of records verified and consumed. F-02: historical paths come from an exhaustive NUL-safe raw history stream (`git log --all --root --raw --no-renames -m -z --no-abbrev --pretty=format:%x00`), one record per path occurrence per commit, including root and merge commits and historical gitlink entries; `rev-list --objects` representative naming is no longer the path source, so a blob shared by a safe path and a carrier path cannot hide the carrier. F-03: every `cat-file --batch` record must match its request by full id, be a `blob`, carry a non-negative size and end with the record separator; a non-zero exit (even with empty stderr), a short, long, mismatched or truncated stream fails as `HYG-06`. F-04: `SEC-026-10` accepts quoted keys and quoted values across JSON, properties and TOML syntaxes, including quoted values with spaces, with the value-group offset corrected so placeholder suppression is exact. F-05: `HYG-03` covers tracked signing material, environment files and every credential-carrier path alongside the owning `SEC-026-*` finding. F-03 also hardened the shallow, index, `check-ignore` and history parsers to reject malformed or undecodable input instead of skipping it.
- **Validation:** Executed on 2026-09-30 on branch `build/repository-hygiene`, corrected head `963da9c`, JDK 25 daemon, Android SDK platform 37. Clean state: `./gradlew verifyRepositoryHygiene` passes with **95 commit-eligible paths, 704 reachable objects, 326 unique blobs scanned and verified, 101 historical path occurrences classified, 12 credential rule classes, 38 ignore sentinels and 0 findings**; a second run and a configuration-cache reuse both re-execute the task (never `UP-TO-DATE`); `check` and `build` include it; `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds with no `iosApp/`; `validatePlugins --rerun-tasks` succeeds with no warning; `git ls-files -ci --exclude-standard` is empty; all 38 sentinels are ignored and the eight shared Xcode/project paths are not; a fresh clone passes non-shallowly. The original **S1–S14** matrix was re-run — S2 now fails `HYG-02`, `HYG-03` and `SEC-026-12` as originally required — and the added **R1–R15** matrix passed, all in disposable clones: R1 pathless blob (`SEC-026-04`), R2 shared safe/carrier blob (`HYG-04`+`SEC-026-09`), R2b harmless two-path reuse (clean), R3 historical gitlink (`HYG-07`), R4 non-zero batch exit (`HYG-06`, no success count), R5 short and misidentified batch streams (`HYG-06`), R6 quoted JSON assignment (`SEC-026-10`), R7 quoted/unquoted/spaced assignments, R8 placeholder forms (clean), R9 `test`/`dev`/`local` values (still fail), R10 `.env` (`HYG-02`/`HYG-03`/`SEC-026-12`), R11 `release.keystore` (`HYG-02`/`HYG-03`/`SEC-026-09`), R12 nested and non-Git roots (`HYG-05`), R13 paths with spaces, a tab, a newline and non-ASCII (clean), R14 malformed shallow/index/check-ignore records (fail closed), R15 clean self-scan (0 findings). Every seed was removed and the clone returned to a clean pass; no seed value appeared in any output; no seed was committed.
- **Corrects LOG-0036:** LOG-0036's counts (671 objects, 307 blobs) and its claim that the S1–S14 matrix proved every rule describe the earlier implementation at head `e38019a`; the correction above is the current evidence. LOG-0036 is otherwise upheld and is not rewritten (`docs/PROJECT_LOG.md` is append-only).
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so D2 cannot be met and no required check has ever reported here, and branch protection does not yet require the future `repository-hygiene` check; the scanner covers its fixed registry and the blobs reachable from fetched local refs, not arbitrary undiscovered secrets, dangling objects or unfetched refs; no product test, app run, iOS build, performance measurement, release, tag, repository-setting change or secret-management action was performed.

### LOG-0038 · 2026-09-30 · TASK-016 second review corrections: direct tree refs, strict streams, streaming content

- **Event:** A second independent review of PR #13 at head `8f26228` found that a local ref pointing **directly to a tree** could expose a forbidden carrier path while `verifyRepositoryHygiene` still reported `0 findings` (G-01); that a `-z` stream truncated at its final terminator, or an unexpected/blank record, was accepted (G-02); that a fixed 256 MiB blob ceiling neither identified the blocked object nor applied to working-set files (G-03); that a violating path containing a control character rendered across several log lines and could drive the terminal (G-04); that counts and evidence called unique paths “occurrences” (G-05); and that `HANDOFF.md`, `README.es.md` and the PR body carried inconsistent claims (G-06). All six were corrected on `build/repository-hygiene` with new commits, and PR #13 was updated. No decision changed: `DEC-062` states the intended contract and the implementation now satisfies it.
- **Rationale:** `REQ-SEC-002` and `AC-REQ-SEC-002-1` are met only by a check that cannot certify an unclassified reachable path. `git log` enumerates paths introduced through commits and is silent about a ref that points at a tree, so a whole class of reachable state could hide a carrier path; and a truncated stream read as complete is the same false guarantee in a different shape.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/hygiene/` (`GitRepository.kt`, `RepositoryHygieneTask.kt`, `HygieneRules.kt`, `HygieneViolation.kt`, new `ContentScanner.kt` and `SafeText.kt`); `docs/PROJECT_LOG.md`; `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `docs/TESTING.md`; `docs/SECURITY.md`; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md`; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md`; `README.md`; `README.es.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-062` (contract unchanged), `DEC-035`, `DEC-046`, `DEC-049`, `DEC-053`, `DEC-054`, `DEC-057`. No new `DEC-###`; `TASK-016` stays `In review`.
- **Corrections.** G-01: refs are enumerated with `for-each-ref` and peeled with `rev-parse --verify --quiet <ref>^{commit}` then `<ref>^{tree}`; every unique ref whose recursively peeled target is a tree is enumerated with recursive, full-tree `ls-tree`, so its paths reach `HYG-04` and its gitlinks reach `HYG-07`, while commit history stays owned by the raw history stream. G-02: a non-empty `-z` stream must end with the record terminator, and each parser validates its exact grammar (working set, index mode/object/stage/path, tracked-ignored, `check-ignore` four-field tuples, reachable-object id/path records, raw-history header/path pairs, direct-tree entries); the object format is read from `git rev-parse --show-object-format` so object ids are validated at 40 or 64 hex characters; blank or unexpected records fail closed. G-03: the fixed 256 MiB ceiling was removed in favour of streaming content in 256 KiB chunks through `ContentScanner`, which carries an unterminated line and keeps the last complete line as window context, deduplicates findings by absolute offset and derives line numbers from the running newline count, so a credential split at a chunk boundary is still detected; only a single line beyond 64 MiB refuses, and it names the object safely. G-04: every rule id, location and reason crosses `SafeText`, which escapes backslash, tab, newline, carriage return and other C0/DEL characters onto one line, so a path cannot forge a finding line or emit a control sequence; R13-style non-ASCII paths stay readable. G-05: the reported count is “unique historical path(s) classified”. G-06: `HANDOFF.md` now says every **positive** original seed behaved as specified, with `HYG-05` supplied by R12, cites `LOG-0038`, and distinguishes unproven **product** criteria from implemented tooling criteria; `README.es.md` mirrors the TASK-015 `Done`/PR #10 row.
- **Corrects LOG-0037:** `LOG-0037` called the aggregate a “historical path occurrence” and implied the first correction round had closed every fail-closed gap; in fact the count was already deduplicated by path, and a `-z` stream truncated at its final terminator, an unexpected history record, an extra blank classification line and a direct tree ref were still accepted. `LOG-0037` is otherwise upheld and is not rewritten (`docs/PROJECT_LOG.md` is append-only).
- **Validation:** Executed on 2026-09-30 on branch `build/repository-hygiene`; the corrected implementation is commit `f9ff07f` and the current head is the documentation commit that follows it. Clean state at the tested head: `./gradlew verifyRepositoryHygiene` passes with 97 commit-eligible paths, 730 reachable objects, 341 unique blobs streamed and verified, 104 unique historical paths classified, 12 credential rule classes, 38 ignore sentinels and 0 findings; `check` and `build` include it; `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds with no `iosApp/`; `validatePlugins --rerun-tasks` succeeds with no warning; a fresh clone passes non-shallowly. Matrices, all in disposable clones: the prior **S1–S14** and **R1–R15** matrices were re-run and all passed, and the new **T1–T14** matrix passed — T1 direct tree ref with `release.keystore` (`HYG-04`+`SEC-026-09`), T2 shared blob at a safe and a carrier path inside one direct tree (carrier still fails), T3 a two-layer annotated tag chain peeling to the tree (carrier still fails), T4 direct tree gitlink (`HYG-07`), T5 direct tree with allowed paths only (clean), T6 the final terminator removed from each of seven `-z` families (each fails `HYG-06`), T7 an unexpected raw-history record (`HYG-04`, fail closed), T8 an extra blank classification line (`HYG-06`), T9 a malformed index record and a malformed direct-tree record (each fails closed), T10 and T11 an ~11 MiB working-set file and an ~11 MiB history blob with a credential near the end (`SEC-026-10`, no content printed), T11b a large blob with no credential (clean), T12 a credential straddling and T12b a keyword straddling a 256 KiB chunk boundary (`SEC-026-10`), T13 newline/tab/CR/ANSI paths rendering as one escaped deterministic line with no raw control byte and an ordinary non-ASCII path left readable, T14 the clean self-scan with the detector present. Every seed was reverted and the clone returned to a clean pass; no seed value appeared in any output; no seed was committed.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so D2 cannot be met and no required check has ever reported here, and branch protection does not yet require the future `repository-hygiene` check; the scanner covers its fixed registry and the blobs reachable from fetched local refs, not arbitrary undiscovered secrets, dangling objects or unfetched refs; no product test, app run, iOS build, performance measurement, release, tag, repository-setting change or secret-management action was performed.

### LOG-0040 · 2026-10-01 · The remaining work is delivered in nine execution blocks (DEC-063)

- **Event:** With `TASK-016` merged and `Done` (`LOG-0039`), the project moved from individual task execution to a block-based model (`DEC-063`). `docs/BACKLOG.md` §2.6 now owns nine execution blocks `B1`…`B9`, and every task table in §3–§6 gained a `Block` column: B1 Definition and Governance and B2 Quality Infrastructure and CI (M0); B3 Shared Core and Data Layer, B4 Design System and Android Shell, B5 Android Feature Implementation and B6 Android Validation and Release (M1); B7 iOS Infrastructure and Shell and B8 iOS Functional Parity and Closure (M2); B9 Stabilisation and Final Delivery (M3). `TASK-014`…`TASK-016` read `Done` in the column, and the eight open-decision rows `TASK-079`…`TASK-086` (§9) read `—` because they are not scheduled delivery work.
- **Rationale:** Delivery has to accelerate without weakening the evidence each task owes, and the per-task start overhead is the part that can be removed rather than the review. Work that shares a working set is scheduled together so the harness, the contracts and the shared core are built once instead of being re-opened per task, while the unit of progress stays the task: a block is an execution grouping, it never spans a milestone, and each member still lands as its own pull request under its own `TASK-###` with its own TDD cycle (DEC-053) and its own required check set (DEC-054). The alternative — one branch per block — was rejected because it would collapse the red/green/refactor commits DEC-053 preserves and would make a single pull request carry unrelated acceptance criteria.
- **Affected artifacts:** `docs/BACKLOG.md` (§2.1 column scheme, §2.4 initial status, §2.5, new §2.6 execution blocks, the `Block` column of the §3–§6 tables, §10 change log); `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md` (`DEC-063`, §5 change log); `docs/PROJECT_LOG.md` (this entry).
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-063` (new process decision; no ADR, because it is a process decision rather than architecture). It builds on `DEC-044` (this file is the canonical work index and its column set is the board's contract), `DEC-046`, `DEC-053` and `DEC-054`, whose obligations it explicitly does not modify. No new `TASK-###` or `TEST-###` id was allocated: the decision re-groups existing ids and changes no task's meaning.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on the working tree. The `Block` column was added to the five task tables by transforming each table row, and every row still carries the same 13 columns with its id, dependencies and status unchanged (86 rows parsed: 3 `Done`, 83 `Proposed`, 11 of them outside the block plan). The dependency graphs of all 75 block-scoped tasks were read from the rows and checked pairwise: the grouping contains **no edge from a task to a task in a later block** (0 forward edges after the adjustments below), so the block order is a topological order of the graph. Four adjustments were required and are recorded in §2.6: `TASK-020` B6→B5 (its dependants `TASK-012` and `TASK-075` are in B5), `TASK-012` B5→B6 (it consumes `TASK-020`, which now sits at the end of B5), `TASK-031` B2→B1 (its dependency `TASK-019` and its dependant `TASK-073` are both in B1), and `TASK-021` added to B4 (the proposed lists omitted it; it is live M1 work whose only dependency is `TASK-005` in B4, and assigning it keeps `REQ-FUNC-021` inside the plan). All four stay inside their milestone. `./gradlew check`, which includes `verifyDependencyPolicy` and `verifyRepositoryHygiene`, was run on the changed tree: it succeeds, `verifyDependencyPolicy` (its `verifyDependencyPins`, `verifyDependencyRationale` and `verifyDependencyInventory` tasks) passes, and `verifyRepositoryHygiene` reports 0 findings over the working set and every reachable blob — so no project file was corrupted by the update. Structural checks were also run over the result: the five tables parse with the same 13 columns per row, the §2.6 block table and the `Block` column agree for every block, 86 task rows and every relative link in the touched documents resolves, and no row lost its id, dependencies or status.
- **Not verified:** the block plan is a scheduling model, not a delivered increment: no block-scoped task has been started, and B1–B9 remain `Proposed`; no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so no required check has ever reported here; and the dependency check is a static read of the `Dependencies` column, not a runtime proof that the block order is sufficient — that proof is the same `TECHNICAL_PLAN.md` §5 constraint set the tasks already cite.

### LOG-0044 · 2026-10-01 · The module-boundary check is executable (TASK-017)

- **Event:** `verifyModuleBoundaries` now exists as the third root policy plugin, `multiverse.module.boundaries` (`build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/boundaries/`), wired into the root `check` and applied only at the root. It turns the accepted module graph of ADR-0001 as amended by ADR-0010, ADR-0012, `DEC-066` and `DEC-069` into rules `R1`–`R14` (project edges, source-set kinds, the `:core:domain` external allow-list, the `:androidApp` composition, `:core:ios`'s accepted edges, fail-closed on an unrecognised module or edge) and `S1`–`S3` (the staged destination/package rules of `DEC-068`, owned by `TASK-017` per that decision).
- **Rationale:** the dependency rules were a review habit, and the two prior policy plugins (`DEC-061`, `DEC-062`) showed the pattern: a policy that only a reviewer remembers decays, and an executable rule cannot be argued around. The check reads each project's **own** declared dependencies from the Gradle model — what `DEC-057` requires in the consuming module's build script — and classifies the configuration and source set that declared each edge, so a violation is attributable to the diff that introduced it. Build tooling that declares project dependencies for its own purposes (Kotlin ABI and build-tools classpaths, compiler-plugin classpaths, Android Lint's tool configuration, the SwiftPM lock-file carrier) is not a module boundary and is not evaluated as one.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/boundaries/` (`ModuleBoundaryRules.kt`, `ModuleGraphSnapshot.kt`, `ModuleBoundaryCapture.kt`, `ModuleBoundaryViolation.kt`, `VerifyModuleBoundariesTask.kt`, `ModuleBoundariesPlugin.kt`); `build-logic/convention/build.gradle.kts` (plugin registration); root `build.gradle.kts` (apply); `docs/DESIGN.md` \u00a73.4; `docs/TESTING.md` \u00a73.2/\u00a73.3/\u00a714.2/\u00a717; `docs/GUIDELINES.md` \u00a71.3/\u00a73.1; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `docs/HANDOFF.md`; this entry.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-052` (the module set), `DEC-057` (edges in the consuming script), `DEC-066` (the `:core:domain` rule), `DEC-068` (the staged `TEST-UNIT-043` ownership), `DEC-061`/`DEC-062` (the plugin pattern). No new id: the check implements `TEST-UNIT-017`, the external half of `TEST-UNIT-012` (`R14`) and `TEST-UNIT-043`.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `build/module-boundary-enforcement`. Clean repository: `./gradlew verifyModuleBoundaries` passes with 14 projects checked, and `./gradlew check build :androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds twice in a row with the second run reusing the configuration cache. Seeds in a disposable worktree, each reverted after the run: a feature→feature edge fails `R7`; a `:core:designsystem`→`:core:domain` edge fails `R4`; a `:core:domain`→`:core:data` edge fails `R1`; a `:core:data`→`:core:presentation` edge fails `R2`; a production `:core:testing` edge fails `R6` (and `R2`); a `:core:designsystem` edge from a shared production configuration fails `R9`; an added unknown module fails `R13`; a new external dependency in `:core:domain` fails `R14`; a feature with production source outside its route declaration fails `S3`; a source that references `NavHost` in code fails `S2` while the same token inside a comment and a string literal does not. The worktree and its temporary branch were removed; `git status` is clean of seeds.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so no required-check run was observed; `buildHealth` remains `TASK-029` work; and the rules describe the accepted graph, so a deliberate ADR-level module change must extend the rule set rather than bypass it.
### LOG-0045 · 2026-10-01 · One `VERSION` source (TASK-018)

- **Event:** the repository gained a single `VERSION` file (`0.1.0`), and the Android `versionName` now derives from it: the application convention plugin reads the file and sets `versionName`, while `versionCode` stays unset because no authoritative source defines a formula for it (`DEC-067`). `verifyDependencyPins` gained rule P9: the file must exist and hold exactly one `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` value with no prefix, suffix, build metadata, surrounding whitespace or second line, and no second version literal (`versionName`, `versionCode`, `version = "…"`) may exist in any build script.
- **Rationale:** `AC-REQ-NFR-006-2` requires one repository source for the application version, and the acceptance was split by `DEC-067` because the real iOS `CFBundleShortVersionString` cannot exist before the iOS app target does: B1 owns the source, its validation and the Android field, and `TASK-051` owns the iOS wiring. A hand-edited platform literal is now a build failure rather than a review finding.
- **Affected artifacts:** `VERSION` (new); `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/ApplicationVersion.kt` (new reader); `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/AndroidApplicationConventionPlugin.kt`; `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/DependencyPinsTask.kt` (P9) and `DependencyPolicyPlugin.kt` (the two new inputs); `README.md` and `README.es.md` §14; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `docs/HANDOFF.md`; this entry.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-043` (one `VERSION`, tag `vMAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`), `DEC-067` (the split acceptance), `AC-REQ-NFR-006-2`, `TEST-UNIT-014`. No new id.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `build/shared-version-source`. `./gradlew verifyDependencyPins` passes with `VERSION` = `0.1.0`; `./gradlew check build :androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds twice with the second run reusing the configuration cache; `aapt2 dump badging` on the built APK reports `versionName='0.1.0'`, equal to the file. Seeds in a disposable worktree, each reverted: a missing `VERSION` fails the build (the application plugin cannot set the field), an empty file, `v1.0.0`, `1.0`, `1.0.0-SNAPSHOT`, a blank second line, surrounding spaces and two lines each fail P9 with their own diagnostic, and a hard-coded `versionName` in `androidApp/build.gradle.kts` fails with `androidApp/build.gradle.kts:21: a second version literal`. The worktree and its branch were removed.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`); the iOS side of the acceptance (`AC-REQ-NFR-006-3`) is target state until `TASK-051`; and `versionCode` has no formula, so the APK reports an empty `versionCode` by design rather than by oversight.

### LOG-0047 · 2026-10-01 · The TDD phase protocol is stated as twelve ordered steps (TASK-072)

- **Event:** `CONTRIBUTING.md` §3.1 now states the protocol as twelve numbered steps — Ready, test first, red, observed failure, green, observed pass, refactor, preserve, push, green final state, review, merge commit — with the three exception classes (pure documentation, build/CI configuration, tooling) and the explicit statement that an exception removes the artificial commit obligation only, never verification, seed tests, documentation or the final gate. §2 states that an open branch is brought up to date by **merging `main`**, never by rebasing a branch whose phase commits are already pushed. `GUIDELINES.md` §8.1 and `DEFINITION.md` D3 reference §3.1 instead of restating the cycle.
- **Rationale:** `REQ-NFR-010` and `AC-REQ-NFR-010-1` require the three commit forms with their phase meaning, the no-squash policy and named exception classes; the documents had the phases but not the ordered protocol, and the merge policy statement lived only in §3.5. Writing it once, in the owner, removes the ambiguity a contributor or agent would otherwise resolve twice.
- **Affected artifacts:** `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` §2/§3.1; `docs/GUIDELINES.md` §8.1; `docs/DEFINITION.md` D3; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; this entry.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-053` (the protocol), `DEC-059` (merge-commit integration), `REQ-NFR-010`, `AC-REQ-NFR-010-1`, `AC-REQ-NFR-010-3`. No new id: the change states what the decisions already fix.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `docs/tdd-phase-protocol`. The documents were read against one another (`AC-REQ-NFR-010-1`…`3`, `DEC-053`, `DEC-059`, `DEFINITION.md` D2–D4, `TESTING.md` §1.1/§14, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `GUIDELINES.md`) and agree: the protocol is twelve steps, the red commit is expected to fail and the gate evaluates the pull request's final state, no pushed phase commit is rewritten, no text prescribes squash or rebase merge, and `TEST-UNIT-041` is described as history/review evidence rather than a substitute for the observed red output. `./gradlew check` passes on the head.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so the protocol is documented but not machine-enforced; `TEST-UNIT-041` remains planned with its traceability row unchanged.
