### LOG-0049 · 2026-10-01 · Post-merge review: the boundary check failed open, the D2 gate had no owner decision (`DEC-071`)

- **Event:** An independent review of the merged Block 1 reached `main` (`3f44a61`) found that Block 1 was **not correctly complete** and that five defects reproduced. Reproduced here in a disposable full clone of `origin/main`, each seed reverted afterwards and the clean tree re-checked: (F1) `:feature:discovery` declared `project(":feature:favorites")` on a custom configuration that `commonMainImplementation` extended, and `verifyModuleBoundaries` still exited 0; (F2) `:core:designsystem` declared `libs.ktor.client.core` and the check still exited 0, although its acceptance is Compose only; (F3) `navigation/CharacterList.kt` was replaced by a constant with no destination and `S1` still accepted it; (F4) `domain/`/`presentation/` files declaring `...settings.wrong` packages satisfied `S3`; (F5) with `VERSION` set to `invalid-version`, `verifyDependencyPins` failed as designed but `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeded and `aapt2 dump badging` reported `versionName='invalid-version'`.
- **Rationale:** The boundary check is the executable form of ADR-0001's rules, so a state it passes while the architecture forbids it is worse than no check: it converts a violation into evidence. The `VERSION` bypass is the same failure in a different shape — the validator existed but the documented artifact command did not consume it, so `AC-REQ-NFR-006-2` was claimed on a path that could not enforce it. The third finding is governance rather than code: `DEFINITION.md` D2 required the complete both-runner check set before a task is `Done`, no CI existed, and the B1 pull requests had empty status rollups, so the closure PR (#37, merge `3f44a61`) had claimed a completion the state model could not support. The owner decided **staged activation** (`DEC-071`), which is recorded here and in the owning documents — `DEC-054`'s unconditional wording is amended, not reinterpreted silently.
- **Affected artifacts:** `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md` (`DEC-071`); `docs/DEFINITION.md` (D2 and its notes); `docs/TESTING.md` §14.2; `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` §5.3; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`CONF-53` resolved, `GAP-012` and `GAP-013` recorded); `docs/BACKLOG.md` (§2.4, §9 rows `TASK-087`…`TASK-090`); this entry. The technical corrections (`TASK-088`, `TASK-089`) and the reconciliation (`TASK-090`) follow in their own pull requests.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-071` (new; amends `DEC-054`'s wording, no ADR because it is a process decision). `DEC-046` and `DEFINITION.md` §6 DOC8 are the authority for the documentation defects. `REQ-NFR-001`, `REQ-NFR-006`, `REQ-NFR-009`, `REQ-NFR-011` are the affected requirements. No published commit was rewritten: the review's findings are corrected by new commits.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 against `origin/main` `3f44a61` in a disposable clone at `/tmp/b1-review-red` (removed after the run). Baseline: `verifyModuleBoundaries` exits 0 and reports 14 projects. F1 exit 0, F2 exit 0, F3 exit 0, F4 exit 0 — each with no diagnostic line; F5 `verifyDependencyPins` exit 1 with `TEST-UNIT-014: VERSION: `invalid-version` is not a `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` value...`, `:androidApp:assembleDebug` exit 0, APK badging `versionName='invalid-version'`. `gh pr view` for PRs #25 and #31–#37 reports `MERGED` with **0** entries in every `statusCheckRollup`. PR #33's merge commit is `f121930` (its feature commit is `0679b31`).
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so no required-check run was observed for any B1 merge and D2 was never evaluable; `TASK-088`, `TASK-089` and `TASK-090` are not implemented by this entry; and Block 1 remains **integrated and locally verified**, not `Done under D2`, until `TASK-025` creates the workflows and the active checks pass.
### LOG-0050 · 2026-10-01 · The module-boundary check stops failing open (`TASK-088`)

- **Event:** `verifyModuleBoundaries` now reads the **effective** dependencies of every architecture-relevant configuration — its own declarations plus everything reachable through `extendsFrom` — instead of only the direct declarations of a hard-coded name set, and it gains rule `R15`: `:core:designsystem` may declare Compose-only external dependencies. The staged structure rules became content-aware: `S1` requires a real typed destination (`@Serializable` + `data object`/`data class`, or a `@Serializable` sealed/enum route per `DESIGN.md` §4.2) rather than a file under `navigation/`, and `S3` requires the feature's actual `.domain`/`.presentation` Kotlin packages rather than directory names. Diagnostics name the effective configuration, the source set, the producer and, when the edge was inherited, the declaring configuration.
- **Rationale:** a check that passes a state the architecture forbids converts a violation into false evidence. The four false negatives reproduced by the post-merge review (`GAP-012`) all came from the same shape: a rule that trusted a name. Reading the Gradle model's effective graph and the source content removes the name-trust in the two dimensions that matter — dependencies and declarations — while the toolchain's implicit `kotlin-stdlib` and the build's own classpaths stay outside the rule on purpose.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/boundaries/` (`ModuleBoundaryCapture.kt`, `ModuleBoundaryRules.kt`, `ModuleGraphSnapshot.kt`, `ModuleBoundaryViolation.kt`, `VerifyModuleBoundariesTask.kt`, new `FeatureSources.kt` and `ModuleDependencyAllowLists.kt`); `docs/TESTING.md` §3.2/§3.3; `docs/DESIGN.md` §3.4; `docs/GUIDELINES.md` §1.3; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; this entry.
- **Decision / ADR:** `TASK-088` correcting `TASK-017`; `DEC-066` and `DEC-068` unchanged; no new decision id. The rule range is now `R1`–`R13` + `R15` + `S1`–`S3`, and every document that states a range was updated in the same change.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `test/module-boundary-review-corrections` in a disposable full clone, each seed reverted and the clean tree re-checked: M1 direct and **M2 inherited** feature→feature both fail `R7`; M3 an inherited external in `:core:domain` fails `R14`; M4 a `:core:designsystem` project dependency fails `R4`; M5 direct and **M6 inherited** Ktor in `:core:designsystem` fail `R15`; M7 a permitted Compose dependency passes; M8 a `navigation/` file with no destination and M9 one whose destination appears only in a comment both fail `S1`; M10 the five real destinations pass; M11 `NavHost` in executable code fails `S2` while M12 the same token in a comment and a string passes; M13 wrong `domain`/`presentation` packages fail `S3` while M14 correct packages pass; M15 an unknown module fails closed (`R13`); and the clean tree passes on a cold run and on a configuration-cache-reused run. Before the correction the same four seeds (F1–F4) exited 0 with no diagnostic.
- **Not verified:** the check has no durable test source set — adding one needs a test dependency the dependency policy does not currently admit — so the seed matrix above is the recorded evidence; no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so `TASK-088` is *integrated and locally verified*, not `Done under D2` until `TASK-025` activates the runner.
### LOG-0051 · 2026-10-01 · Every Android artifact path consumes the `VERSION` validation (`TASK-089`)

- **Event:** the dependency-policy plugin now wires every Android application artifact task — `assemble`, `bundle`, `package`, `install`, `extract` and their per-variant forms — to `verifyDependencyPins`, so the canonical `VERSION` grammar of rule P9 is enforced **before** an artifact can exist and the documented build commands cannot bypass it. The wiring is lazy (`configureEach`), configuration-cache compatible, and names the aggregate task rather than a `Task` instance.
- **Rationale:** a post-merge review reproduced the bypass (`GAP-013`): with `VERSION` set to `invalid-version`, `verifyDependencyPins` failed while `:androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeded and produced an APK whose badging reported `versionName='invalid-version'`. The validator was correct but advisory; the acceptance criterion `AC-REQ-NFR-006-2` is about the artifact, so the artifact path has to consume the validation. `bundleRelease` remains blocked by the pre-existing empty `versionCode`, which no authoritative source or decision defines a formula for (`DEC-067`); the validator now fails it earlier and with the correct diagnostic.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/DependencyPolicyPlugin.kt`; `docs/TESTING.md` §3.3/§18; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; this entry.
- **Decision / ADR:** `TASK-089` correcting `TASK-018`; `DEC-067` (the split acceptance) and `AC-REQ-NFR-006-2` unchanged; no new decision id.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `build/version-validation-review-corrections-fix` in a disposable full clone, each seed reverted and the APK output directory deleted before the run. Valid `0.1.0`: `verifyDependencyPins` 0, `:androidApp:assembleDebug` 0, badging `versionName='0.1.0'`. Malformed seeds — missing, empty, `invalid-version`, `v1.0.0`, `1.0`, `1.0.0-SNAPSHOT`, surrounding spaces, a blank second line, two semantic-version lines and a duplicate hard-coded `versionName` — each returned non-zero from **both** `verifyDependencyPins` and `:androidApp:assembleDebug` and left **no** APK. `:androidApp:assembleRelease` behaves the same way; `:androidApp:bundleRelease` fails at the validator first and then at the pre-existing empty `versionCode`. `./gradlew verifyDependencyPins :androidApp:assembleDebug` passes on the clean tree.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so `TASK-089` is *integrated and locally verified*, not `Done under D2` (`DEC-071`); the iOS `CFBundleShortVersionString` wiring stays with `TASK-051`; and the `versionCode` gap is an open design question, not a regression this correction introduces.

### LOG-0054 · 2026-10-01 · Residual B1 state fragments refreshed after the correction merges (`TASK-090` follow-up)

- **Event:** with the four correction pull requests merged (`#42`–`#45`, then the bookkeeping `#46`), `HANDOFF.md` still carried fragments that predated them: the documentation-system row read *being established* and pointed at the branch `docs/documentation-system`; the decision ranges stopped at `DEC-063`; the priority list described actions 1–4 as B1 work still to do, including *"land the B1 definitions, then add the boundary check (`TASK-017`) and the `VERSION` wiring (`TASK-018`)"*, although both are merged; and the block bullet named the pre-merge `main` SHA. ### LOG-0055 · 2026-10-01 · The boundary check certified a topology that had lost a module (`TASK-091`, `GAP-014`)

- **Event:** a review of the B1 closure reproduced a false negative in `verifyModuleBoundaries` in a disposable full clone: with `:feature:settings` removed from `settings.gradle.kts` and from `:androidApp`, the task exited 0 and reported 13 projects. Every rule was a prohibition — an edge, a dependency, a source file — and a build that has lost a module has less to prohibit, so the gate certified a repository whose accepted topology no longer existed. The same review showed `moduleKindOf` classified any `:feature:*` path as a valid feature, so an invented feature inherited every feature rule instead of failing closed.
- **Rationale:** `TASK-014` and ADR-0001 fix the module set, and `TASK-017` claims to make module boundaries a build property; a gate that accepts deletion or arbitrary feature names does not enforce that topology (`AC-REQ-NFR-009-3`). `R16` is the check's first rule that can fail because something is **absent**.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/boundaries/ModuleSet.kt` (new: the canonical leaf set, the accepted five features, the planned `:core:ios`, the containers); `ModuleBoundaryRules.kt` (`R16`, the narrowed `:core:ios` allow-list, the consolidated domain allow-list); `ModuleGraphSnapshot.kt` (classification); `VerifyModuleBoundariesTask.kt` (rule range); `docs/DESIGN.md` §3.4; `docs/TESTING.md` §3.3/§14; `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`GAP-014`); `docs/BACKLOG.md` (`TASK-091`).
- **Decision / ADR:** no new decision; applies `DEC-046`, `REQ-NFR-001`/`009` and ADR-0001 as amended by ADR-0010/ADR-0012.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `test/module-topology-hardening`, from `origin/main` `5aa9128`. Red phase (`3c9ce9a`): the new `ModuleTopologyTest` ran 8 tests with 5 failures because `R16` did not exist and `:feature:invented` classified as a feature. Green phase (`dd05297`): the same 8 tests pass, and in a disposable clone removing `:feature:settings` now fails `R16` with a diagnostic naming the module while a fully-formed `:feature:invented` fails `R13`.
- **Not verified:** no CI exists (`GAP-002`), so the change is *integrated and locally verified*, not `Done under D2` (`DEC-071`). `:core:ios` is not promoted to required here — `TASK-078` owns that, and its export set is the open `CONF-54`.

### LOG-0056 · 2026-10-01 · The build-logic policy gained a durable regression suite (`TASK-092`, `GAP-015`)

- **Event:** no `*Test.kt` existed outside generated build output, so every boundary, dependency and hygiene rule was proved only by manual seed matrices recorded in prose. That is exactly how `GAP-012` and `GAP-014` were each reproducible while the clean repository kept passing. `build-logic/convention/src/test` now holds the suite and the root `check` runs it.
- **Rationale:** a Gradle verification task is executable policy, but it is not a regression suite for the implementation of that policy; a later edit to a parser, a classifier, a capture rule or the plugin wiring could make a rule fail open silently. `DEC-071` also requires a suite to become mandatory in the same change that creates its harness.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/build.gradle.kts` (test source set: the already-pinned `kotlin-test`/`junit4` and Gradle's bundled TestKit); `src/test/kotlin/**` (the boundary rules `R1`–`R16`, the staged rules `S1`–`S3`, the source mask and destination/NavHost detection, the policy parsers, the hygiene path/content rules, `ApplicationVersion`, and TestKit fixtures for plugin registration, the root-`check` dependency, the artifact→`VERSION` wiring and configuration-cache reuse); `build.gradle.kts` (the lazy `check` dependency on the included build); `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/DependencyPolicyPlugin.kt` (test sources are not build logic, so the policy no longer scans its own suite); `README.md`/`README.es.md` (the inventory records `kotlin-test`/`junit4` as declared by `:build-logic:convention`); `docs/TESTING.md` §14.
- **Decision / ADR:** no new decision; applies `DEC-071`, `REQ-NFR-005`/`009`.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `test/module-topology-hardening`, from `origin/main` `5aa9128`: `./gradlew check` runs `:build-logic:convention:test` (observed through `--dry-run`), the suite reports 59 tests with 0 failures, and mutation-testing the artifact-family regex makes the wiring test fail while the same edit leaves the clean repository passing. A run of the suite opens no socket, reads no clock and asserts no machine path.
- **Not verified:** the suite is not yet a required check on either runner: CI is `TASK-025` (`GAP-002`), so `DEC-071` applies and the task stays *In review* until a human merges it and a later change activates the gate.

### LOG-0058 · 2026-10-01 · A closeout commit reached `main` through the branch ruleset's bypass actor

- **Event:** the `TASK-091`/`TASK-092` closeout document (`TASK-092`'s post-merge bookkeeping) was committed as `b78662a` on 2026-10-01 and pushed with `git push origin main`. The push was **not rejected**: GitHub returned the branch-ruleset bypass notice, and the commit entered `main` directly. The repository's `main protection` ruleset is active and lists the `pull_request` rule, but its `bypass_actors` configures actor `davidru85` with `bypass_mode: always`, so the rule is not enforced against the credentials that performed the push. `GET /repos/davidru85/RickAndMorty/commits/b78662a/pulls` returns 0 associated pull requests.
- **Rationale:** `AGENTS.md` §4.2 prohibits merging, force-pushing and rewriting history without an explicit human instruction, and `DEC-049` reserves merge and repository-setting actions to a human. A direct push to the default branch bypasses both the review trail the repository's workflow requires (`AGENTS.md` §5) and the `pull_request` rule the ruleset states. The agent misread the bypass notice as a rejection and proceeded; the sequence is wrong even though the content was documentation-only and the gate was green.
- **Affected artifacts:** `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `docs/PROJECT_LOG.md`; this entry. No source, build or behaviour artifact is involved.
- **Decision / ADR:** no new decision; applies `DEC-046`, `DEC-049` and `AGENTS.md` §4.2. The corrective action is a matter of process, not of architecture.
- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 from `origin/main` `b78662a`: the commit is the branch tip, `commits/b78662a/pulls` is empty, the ruleset reads `rules: [deletion, non_fast_forward, pull_request]` with `bypass: [{actor_id: 472324, bypass_mode: always}]`, and the commit's parents are `[9cf6217]` — the `TASK-091`/`TASK-092` merge — so it is a direct child of the merged pull request rather than of a review merge. The remedy reverts the unreviewed commit and re-lands the identical content through a pull request, so the merged history distinguishes the reviewed artifact from the bypassed one.
- **Not verified:** whether the ruleset bypass is intended. Removing or narrowing `bypass_actors` is a repository-settings change and stays a human action (`DEC-049`); the agent did not read or modify it. Until then, no credential used by an agent should push to `main`, and every closeout goes through branch plus pull request.

- **Event:** `TASK-024` delivers the shared test harness in `:core:testing`: twenty fixtures captured from the live API on 2026-10-01, each with a machine-readable `.meta.json` sidecar recording its source, capture date, HTTP status, `sha256` and the shape it pins; the single `FixtureLoader` seam; the `MockEngine` harness; the `TestDispatcher` and injected-clock helpers; and `TEST-UNIT-024`, a live-host guard that fails closed on any live host literal in a test source set. `TASK-025` adds `.github/workflows/pull-request.yml`, the blocking gate on an Ubuntu and a macOS runner with every action pinned by full commit SHA. `TASK-028` adds `TEST-UNIT-044`, which verifies that the required set is present and blocking in the workflow configuration, and places the quarantine register in `TESTING.md` §15.1.

- **Validation:** Observed on 2026-10-01 on branch `test/core-testing-harness` (PR #52): 7 harness tests pass on the JVM host-test target and on `iosSimulatorArm64`; 69 build-logic tests pass; `./gradlew check build :androidApp:assembleDebug` succeeds; the fixture embedding round-trips byte-for-byte (the generated source's content hashes equal its sidecar's `sha256`); the live-host guard was observed red on a seeded offender and green once removed, and its first implementation was found to silently pass the seed — fail-open — which is why its file-tree patterns were widened and it gained both-direction tests; `verifyWorkflowGate` passes on the real workflow and fails on a synthesised one that omits a job, omits a check, neutralises a check or unpins an action.
- **Not verified:** the both-runner CI run on this pull request was still executing when this entry was written; until both jobs are green, the three tasks stay *In review* rather than `Done under D2`. Branch protection is a repository setting and remains a human action (`DEC-049`); the configuration a maintainer must apply is recorded in `CONTRIBUTING.md` §5.3. A defect fixed in this change: the KMP library plugin did not enable the Android host-test target, so the shared `commonTest` sources compiled nowhere on the Linux runner and the gate would have reported a green suite that executed no test.

### LOG-0065 · 2026-10-02 · Block 2 closes for good: the last residue is reconciled (`TASK-097`)

- **Event:** the closure audit of `TASK-095`/`TASK-096` found the residue that kept the block from satisfying its own §11.2: six current-state claims that described a pre-gate repository (`HANDOFF.md` §6's "no workflow exists", §1.1/§7's unrun formatter list, §8's Roborazzi attribution, `TECHNICAL_PLAN.md` §2.1's "empty-but-executing test set", `DEFINITION.md`/`CONTRIBUTING.md`'s "until CI exists", `DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` §5's "no CI"), §14.2's rows not naming their activation task, six blank lines breaking the `BACKLOG.md` tables, `CONF-51` still listed as open although `DEC-075` resolved it, and an orphan `TASK-###` in the audit change log.
- **Rationale:** `DEC-046` makes a document that contradicts the repository a defect, and `DEC-071`'s staged activation is only honest if each row says which task activates it. A block cannot be called closed while its own documents describe a different repository.
- **Affected artifacts:** `AGENTS.md`; both READMEs; `docs/{HANDOFF,TECHNICAL_PLAN,DEFINITION,CONTRIBUTING,TESTING,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,BACKLOG,PROJECT_LOG}.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** none — reconciliation inside `DEC-046`/`DEC-071`.
- **Validation:** `./gradlew check` green on the branch; 97 `TASK-###` rows, all unique, no blank line inside a table; 0 broken relative links; no document now claims a formatter or the gate is absent.
- **Not verified:** the cron trigger of the live workflow (first Monday 06:00 UTC); branch protection still names no required check (`DEC-049`).

### LOG-0066 · 2026-10-02 · The workflow gate reads structure, not text (`TASK-098`)

- **Event:** `GAP-016` recorded that `WorkflowGateGuard` certified configurations in which required verification could not run. The audit reproduced four bypasses on `8e837e1` with **zero findings**: `if: ${{ false }}` on both required jobs, every `./gradlew` replaced by `echo ./gradlew`, a gate reachable only by `workflow_dispatch`, and `on: [pull_request, push]` (flow sequence) with a live-mode invocation added to an ordinary step. The root cause was structural: the guard searched the concatenated text of every workflow, so it could not tell which job, event or step a string belonged to.
- **Rationale:** `AC-REQ-NFR-011-1` claims that the required checks block every pull request; a guard that cannot express "this command is an executable step of the `android` job, on a pull-request-triggered workflow" cannot support that claim. Under `DEC-071` a check that exists is mandatory, so the gate that certifies the check must be as strong as the check itself.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/{WorkflowGateGuard,WorkflowDocument}.kt`; `build-logic/convention/src/test/kotlin/policy/{WorkflowGateGuardTest,WorkflowGateReproductionTest}.kt`; `build-logic/convention/build.gradle.kts`; `gradle/libs.versions.toml`; `README.md`; `README.es.md`; `docs/{DESIGN,TESTING,BACKLOG,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** none — remediation inside `DEC-054`, `DEC-071` and the `TEST-UNIT-044` id. The YAML parser is a new pinned dependency (`libs.snakeyaml.engine`, `2.10`) with its `DESIGN.md` §3.5 rationale and inventory rows; it was already on the build's own classpath transitively through the ktlint plugin, so the pin changes no resolved version and replaces a silent transitive with a declared one (`REQ-NFR-002`).
- **Validation:** red → green observed on 2026-10-02 in the working clone. **Red:** with the previous guard in place, the two suites report **19 failures of 36**, including all four `GAP-016` reproductions (`WorkflowGateReproductionTest`) and the fixture matrix (comment, step `name`, `env`, `echo`, `--dry-run`, `-x`, `|| true`, skip condition, unrelated job, absent `pull_request`, narrowed `pull_request`, malformed YAML, runner-by-identity). **Green:** `./gradlew -p build-logic :convention:check` succeeded; `./gradlew check` succeeded (387 tasks, 352 executed); `./gradlew verifyWorkflowGate` reports "the required-check set is present and blocking (`TEST-UNIT-044`)" against the repository's own workflows, which pass unchanged; `verifyDependencyPolicy` is green with the new pin, rationale row and both inventory rows.
- **Not verified:** the guard has not been exercised on a GitHub runner in this change; the workflow itself is unchanged, so the observation recorded in `LOG-0058`…`LOG-0065` still applies. The scheduled live job's cron trigger has still to fire (first Monday 06:00 UTC).

### LOG-0067 · 2026-10-02 · The documented local gate tells the truth, and proves it (`TASK-103`)

- **Event:** `GAP-020` recorded that `README.md` §9's "All shared and unit tests" row documented `./gradlew test`, which selects the Android unit-test tasks only; observed with `./gradlew test --dry-run`: 4 test tasks, all Android, and neither `:core:testing:testAndroidHostTest` nor `:core:testing:iosSimulatorArm64Test`. `verifyDocumentedGate` could not see it, because it resolved a documented token by registered name *or* by path suffix and compared only each aggregate's direct dependencies.
- **Rationale:** `AC-REQ-NFR-007-1` makes the documented command list the local gate a reviewer copies. A row that names a command proving nothing is a false claim, and a verifier that accepts it turns the documentation into the defect it was written to prevent (`DEC-046`).
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/{VerifyDocumentedGateTask,DependencyPolicyPlugin}.kt`; `build-logic/convention/src/test/kotlin/boundaries/DocumentedGatePluginFunctionalTest.kt`; `README.md`; `README.es.md`; `docs/{TESTING,BACKLOG,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** none — remediation inside `TEST-UNIT-015`, `AC-REQ-NFR-007-1`, `DEC-047` and `DEC-071`. No dependency and no interface changed.
- **Validation:** red → green observed on 2026-10-02 in the working clone. **Red:** with the shipped verifier and plugin, the new functional suite reports **5 failures of 5**; each fixture is a `GAP-020` bypass. Seeds observed against the new verifier on the real repository: a documented task that does not exist is reported; removing the root `check` dependency that carried the build-logic suite is reported ("does not reach `:build-logic:convention:test`"); a row that echoes its command is reported; a Spanish row whose command diverges from the English one is reported ("the two tables must stay aligned"); the unseeded repository passes. **Green:** `./gradlew -p build-logic :convention:check` green (14 s); 107 build-logic tests, 0 failures; `./gradlew check` green (19 s); `verifyRepositoryHygiene` 0 findings after a constant named `…TOKEN` was renamed — `SEC-026-10` read it as a credential assignment.
- **Not verified:** the guard has not been exercised on a GitHub runner in this change. The fixture cannot reproduce the repository's own claim table (it names the repository's suites), so the claim-coverage rule is proved by the seeds above rather than by the functional fixture.

### LOG-0068 · 2026-10-02 · The Swift quality gate stops failing open (`TASK-099`)

- **Event:** `GAP-023` recorded four defects in `tools/swift-lint.sh` and `tools/swift-tools-setup.sh`: with a nonempty input set and neither tool resolvable the gate printed warnings and exited **0**, provisioning accepted any nonempty `xcrun` answer against the Xcode 27.0 lock, an override could bypass the pins, and an unquoted `$FILES` expansion broke paths containing whitespace.
- **Rationale:** the iOS half of `REQ-NFR-007` was reporting success while inspecting nothing, and `DEC-076`'s pins were documented rather than enforced. A quality gate that fails open is worse than an absent one, because it is cited as evidence.
- **Affected artifacts:** `tools/swift-lint.sh`; `tools/swift-tools-setup.sh`; `tools/swift-tools-test.sh` (new, `TEST-UNIT-046`); `docs/{TESTING,BACKLOG,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** none — remediation inside `DEC-076` and `TEST-UNIT-046`. No dependency changed; swift-format is read from the Xcode that ships it, established from `xcodebuild -version`.
- **Validation:** red → green observed on 2026-10-02 on macOS 26 / Xcode 27.0 / bash 3.2. **Red (reproduced faithfully in an isolated fixture carrying the shipped script):** `.swiftlint` unresolvable, PATH stripped, cache pointed at an empty directory, one clean Swift file → the shipped script printed `WARN: swiftlint was not found; the lint half did not run.` and exited **0**. Against the new suite the shipped scripts fail 2 of 9. **Green:** `tools/swift-tools-test.sh` reports 9 passed, 0 failed; `tools/swift-tools-setup.sh` downloads the pinned SwiftLint, verifies its SHA-256 and reports `Xcode 27.0 verified`; `tools/swift-lint.sh` runs the real provisioned SwiftLint over a fixture source tree (`Found 0 violations`) and exits 0; a tampered checksum fails before extraction; a `DEVELOPER_DIR` other than the pinned toolchain fails.
- **Not verified:** the gate has not been exercised on a GitHub runner in this change — the Swift sources it will inspect land with `TASK-051`, and `iosApp/` does not exist yet, so the end-to-end run used a fixture source tree. The `ios` job does not call either script yet.

### LOG-0069 · 2026-10-02 · The contract replay proves what actually ran, per target (`TASK-100`)

- **Event:** `GAP-017` recorded that `verifyContractCases` counted `<testcase>` elements without reading `skipped`/`failures`/`errors`, combined both targets into one total, and that the replay filter was applied with a cast to the JVM `org.gradle.api.tasks.testing.Test`, so the Kotlin/Native simulator task ran the whole module's tests unfiltered.
- **Rationale:** the entry point exists so that a green run cannot be green with nothing behind it (`DEC-071`). A skipped-only report counted as one executed case, and a passing JVM run standing in for a missing native report, both defeat that purpose on the one check that certifies the remote contract (`AC-REQ-NFR-011-2`).
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/testing/VerifyContractCasesTask.kt`; `core/data/build.gradle.kts`; `build-logic/convention/src/test/kotlin/testing/ContractTestEntryPointTest.kt`; `docs/{TESTING,BACKLOG,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** none — remediation inside `DEC-073`, `DEC-054` and the `TEST-CONTRACT-*` id. No dependency added.
- **Validation:** red → green observed on 2026-10-02. **Red:** with the shipped verifier, the new fixtures report **2 failures of 5** (`a skipped contract case is not counted as executed`, `a target that produced no report fails even when another target passed`). **Green:** 110 build-logic tests, 0 failures; `./gradlew check` green. The filter was proved with a temporary probe case carrying a deliberately failing second test: under `:core:data:contractTestReplay` only `TEST-CONTRACT-001 …` executed (report `tests=1`), and the probe was removed before the commit; `:core:data:verifyContractCases` then named the missing native report rather than passing on the JVM evidence alone.
- **Not verified:** the Kotlin/Native simulator target has no test source in `:core:data` yet, so the per-target rule is proved by the JVM path plus a fixture that declares both targets, not by a native execution on this machine in this change. `TASK-037` lands the cases that activate the row in CI.

### LOG-0070 · 2026-10-02 · The exclusion register decides per edge, from the build model (`TASK-105`)

- **Event:** `GAP-022` recorded that `verifyDependencyAdviceRegister` diverged from `DEC-077` and from `TASK-029`'s acceptance: it waited for a textual package reference rather than for the edge's own consumer, derived the package of a module from Gradle path spelling — yielding `…feature.character-detail` where `:feature:character-detail` declares `…feature.characterdetail` — ignored test consumption, and treated missing package information as "unused". `CONF-62` recorded the conflict; the owner decided per-edge consumption (`DEC-081`).
- **Rationale:** the register is per edge (`module|dependency|removal-task`), and a per-module expiry contradicts the granularity the file already models. Treating an unknown package as unused is the one direction that silently keeps a stale exclusion alive, which is what the check exists to prevent.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/DependencyAdviceRegisterTask.kt`; `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/policy/DependencyPolicyPlugin.kt`; `build-logic/convention/src/test/kotlin/policy/DependencyAdviceRegisterFunctionalTest.kt`; `docs/{TESTING,BACKLOG,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-081` (per-edge consumption, resolving `CONF-62`), recorded by the owner on 2026-10-02. No dependency changed.
- **Validation:** observed on 2026-10-02. The guard passes the repository's own 24-entry register. Seeds observed: a synthetic import of `:core:data`'s package in `:core:testing` makes that edge stale ("already consumes `:core:data` in its production source"); the same for `:feature:character-detail|:core:domain`, the module whose hyphenated path the old derivation got wrong, now matched through `libraryNamespace()`; an entry naming a non-existent module, a malformed entry, and an entry whose removal task is already `Done` each fail. `DependencyAdviceRegisterFunctionalTest` reports 6 of 6 green; `./gradlew check` green with 116 build-logic tests and 0 failures.
- **Not verified:** the register still holds 24 entries for edges whose consuming tasks have not landed — the placeholder state `DEC-077` exists for. `:core:domain` and `:core:designsystem` have no source yet, so their exclusions are live by the placeholder state rather than by an observation of their consumers.

- **Event:** `GAP-018` recorded that `DEC-079`'s opt-in JVM target was read as a generic project property with no eligibility rule, that boundary snapshots captured no target set, and that the live compilation inherited the daemon's bytecode target. Reproduced: `multiverse.jvmTarget=true` in `:feature:settings` created a JVM target while `verifyModuleBoundaries` passed, and `:core:data`'s contractLive classes carried class-file major version **69** where the contract documents **61**.

The check certified a repository whose accepted topology no longer existed, and a verification artifact was compiled for the wrong JVM.
- **Affected artifacts:** `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/boundaries/{ModuleGraphSnapshot,ModuleBoundaryCapture,ModuleBoundaryRules}.kt`; `build-logic/convention/src/main/kotlin/KmpLibraryConventionPlugin.kt`; `build-logic/convention/src/test/kotlin/boundaries/ModuleBoundaryRulesTest.kt`; `docs/{TESTING,BACKLOG,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`.

- **Validation:** red → green observed on 2026-10-02. **Red:** with `R17` removed from the evaluation, the seven new fixtures report **5 failures of 21** in `ModuleBoundaryRulesTest`. **Seeds on the real repository:** `multiverse.jvmTarget=true` in `:feature:settings` now fails with `R17 … only a `:core:*` KMP library may declare` (the reproduced bypass); the same file removed, `verifyModuleBoundaries` passes over 14 projects; `:core:data:compileContractLiveKotlin` produces class-file major version **61** (was 69 observed before the fix). **Green:** `./gradlew -p build-logic :convention:check` green; `./gradlew check` green (21 fixture tests in `ModuleBoundaryRulesTest`, 7 of them new).

### LOG-0072 · 2026-10-02 · The macOS runner stops proving less than the Linux one (`TASK-102`)

- **Event:** `GAP-019` recorded that the `ios` job compiled and simulated four `:core:*` modules only, that the five existing `:feature:*` modules had **no** native coverage, and that the job omitted the build-logic regression suite, the formatting/static checks and the blocking dependency analysis that `TESTING.md` §14.2 requires on both runners.

The five feature tasks and the ten Apple compile tasks resolve (`--dry-run`) and execute: `:feature:discovery:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` succeeds, and `:core:testing:iosSimulatorArm64Test` runs with `--rerun-tasks`. **Seed:** a deliberate unresolved reference in `:feature:discovery`'s common source fails `:feature:discovery:compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64`, which the shipped job never invoked — the comparison is recorded in the pull request (`0` `:feature:*` references before, `5` modules after). `./gradlew -p build-logic :convention:check`, `./gradlew ktlintCheck`, `./gradlew buildHealth`, `./gradlew check` and `verifyWorkflowGate` are all green locally.

The feature modules have no test source yet, so their `iosSimulatorArm64Test` is `NO-SOURCE` today; the compile step is what covers them until their tests land.

### LOG-0073 · 2026-10-02 · The live observation path is bounded, durable and typed (`TASK-104`)

- **Event:** `GAP-021` recorded six defects in the only path allowed to reach the network: no connect or request timeout, persistence only after the whole loop, a broad transport-failure handler, the capture written before the response was validated, any non-string non-null `info.next` classified as a number, and the validation-error observation `TESTING.md` §11.1 requires missing entirely.
- **Rationale:** the probe output is evidence a human uses to refresh fixtures (`TESTING.md` §11.4) and to detect a contract change (§11.3). A run that can hang, lose its earlier observations, capture a non-JSON body as if it were data, or label a shape change as a number is evidence that misleads the person reading it.
- **Affected artifacts:** `core/data/src/contractLive/kotlin/io/github/davidru85/multiverse/data/live/{ObservationProbes,ObservationRecordOfflineTest}.kt`; `docs/{TESTING,BACKLOG,DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** none — remediation inside `DEC-074` and `TEST-CONTRACT-006`. No dependency changed; the probes still use `java.net.http`.
- **Validation:** red → green observed on 2026-10-02. **Red:** with the shipped classifier restored, `ObservationRecordOfflineTest` reports **2 failures of 11** (`an object next is classified as an object rather than a number`, `an array next …`). **Green:** 11 cases, 0 failures; `./gradlew :core:data:contractLiveTest` green; `./gradlew check` green; `ktlintJvmContractLiveSourceSetCheck` green after the source was formatted with `ktlintFormat`.
- **Not verified:** the probes were not run against the live service in this change — they run only from the scheduled job (`schedule` and `workflow_dispatch`), and the cron trigger has still to fire. The offline suite pins the rules, not the service's current answers.

### LOG-0074 · 2026-10-02 · The platform enforcement of the gate is recorded, and pending (`TASK-106`)

- **Event:** the readiness audit found that the `main` ruleset requires pull-request delivery, merge-commit delivery, deletion and force-push protection, but names **no** required status check, so the required set of `TESTING.md` §14.2 is not enforced by the platform; its single standing bypass actor (`davidru85`, `bypass_mode: always`) is the account used for agent work.
- **Rationale:** `AC-REQ-NFR-007-2` requires a failing, skipped or absent check to block approval. A workflow that runs the checks and a ruleset that ignores their result is the gap between "implemented" and "enforced" (`DEC-071`), and the bypass turns the one account that opens most changes into the one the ruleset cannot bind.
- **Affected artifacts:** `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` §5.3; `docs/{TESTING,BACKLOG,PROJECT_LOG,HANDOFF}.md`. No code, workflow or dependency changed.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-049` (repository settings are human-only) and `DEC-071` (staged activation). Nothing was applied: the section records the observed state and the packet.
- **Validation:** observed on 2026-10-02 through the GitHub REST API with `gh api`: ruleset `24241444` (`main protection`, active, `refs/heads/main`), rules `deletion`/`non_fast_forward`/`pull_request`, no `required_status_checks` rule, `allowed_merge_methods: ["merge"]`, one `bypass_actors` entry (`actor_id 472324`, `User`, `always`). Enforcement observed read-only: pull request #95 (no reported checks) reads `mergeable: MERGEABLE`, `mergeStateStatus: CLEAN`. Repository merge methods: merge `true`, squash `false`, rebase `false`.
- **Not verified:** nothing was applied, so no post-change enforcement state exists to observe; no merge was attempted (`DEC-049`, `AGENTS.md` §4.2). The packet's item 2 is the read-only observation a maintainer makes after applying item 1.

### LOG-0075 · 2026-10-02 · B2's remediation is reconciled against its own audit (`TASK-107`)

- **Event:** the current-state documents did not carry the remediation findings, contained independently verifiable inconsistencies, and overstated B2's completeness: the audit counted the ten `B2-R01`…`B2-R10` findings as open gaps even after their fixes, `HANDOFF.md` said "B2 is closed" and read `TASK-097` as pending rather than merged, and the `B2-R0x` findings had no single traceability table.
- **Rationale:** `DEC-046` makes a document that contradicts the repository a defect. A block cannot be called closed while its own audit's findings are unresolved, and a finding is not resolved merely because a branch exists — the state column says `In review` until the change merges.
- **Affected artifacts:** `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (§6.2.1 traceability, §8 counts); `docs/HANDOFF.md` (§1.2, §4); `docs/BACKLOG.md`; `docs/PROJECT_LOG.md`.
- **Decision / ADR:** none — reconciliation inside `DEC-046` and `DEC-071`.
- **Validation:** the counts were recomputed from the rows: §6.2 now reports **8 open gaps** (`GAP-001`, `GAP-003`…`GAP-008`, `GAP-010`, `GAP-011`) and the ten `B2-R0x` findings are no longer counted among them; all eight gap rows of the remediation (`GAP-016`…`GAP-023`) carry their corrective task; the traceability table in §6.2.1 maps each finding to its task, test family, evidence and state; `TASK-097` reads `PR #76, merged as 8e837e1`; `verifyDocumentedGate`, `verifyRepositoryHygiene` and `./gradlew check` are green. The stale "B2 is closed except `TASK-027`" statement is gone; both READMEs already distinguished deterministic fixture/replay verification from the scheduled live observation and stated the `TASK-037` activation, and `TESTING.md` §11.1 already called a fixture/replay detection a gate failure rather than a signal, so those three needed no change and were verified rather than edited.
- **Not verified:** every corrective change is `In review`, not merged, so no finding is `Remediated` in the merged sense; the state column records that. The repository-settings packet (`TASK-106`) is applied by a maintainer and remains pending (`DEC-049`).

- **Sequential-merge verification:** the eleven changes form a strict ancestor chain, so `main` takes them one at a time. Each of the ten remediation commits was checked out in turn and the **full** local gate run against it (`./gradlew check`): every one reports `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, so no intermediate state of `main` is red and no later change is required to repair an earlier one. The chain's linearity was confirmed with `git merge-base --is-ancestor` on all nine adjacent pairs.

### LOG-0076 · 2026-10-02 · The B2 remediation is merged, and the platform now enforces the gate (`TASK-098`…`TASK-107`)

- **Event:** the B2 audit reproduced ten defects in the merged block (`GAP-016`…`GAP-023`, plus the repository-settings row and the final reconciliation) and registered them as `TASK-098`…`TASK-107`. All ten are now merged, and the repository settings that make the required set enforceable were applied by the maintainer.
- **Rationale:** a green rollup means nothing when the checks cannot fail, the modules are not covered, or the merge actor can bypass them. Each corrective task closed one reproduced bypass with a red → green pair, and the settings change turned the gate from a convention into the platform's rule.
- **Affected artifacts:** the ten corrective changes (see `docs/BACKLOG.md` `TASK-098`…`TASK-107`); `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` §5.3 (the applied settings state and the observed enforcement); `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (§6.2.1 traceability, §8 counts); `docs/HANDOFF.md`; `docs/BACKLOG.md`; this entry.
- **Decision / ADR:** `DEC-080` (module eligibility for the JVM opt-in) and `DEC-081` (per-edge exclusion consumption, resolving `CONF-62`), both taken by the owner from the packets; `DEC-049` kept the settings human-only.
- **Validation:** observed on 2026-10-02 on merged `main` `a1de389`. `./gradlew check` green; `verifyModuleBoundaries` 14 projects, `verifyWorkflowGate`, `verifyDependencyPolicy`, `verifyNoLiveHosts`, `verifyDocumentedGate` and `verifyRepositoryHygiene` (0 findings, 2013 objects, 870 blobs) all pass; the `GAP-018` bypass (a `multiverse.jvmTarget` opt-in in `:feature:settings`) still fails `R17`. The merged content is identical to the reviewed stack: `git diff` between each replacement pull request (#98–#102) and the original it replaced (#93–#97) is empty, and the intermediate `chore: trigger ci` commits change no file. **Enforcement:** the ruleset now carries `required_status_checks` with the contexts `android` and `ios` (bound to the GitHub Actions app, `integration_id 15368`), `strict_required_status_checks_policy: false`, and **no bypass actors**; a disposable pull request with no reported checks read `mergeStateStatus: BLOCKED` (was `CLEAN` before the change), and was then closed and its branch deleted.
- **Not verified:** the scheduled live contract job's cron trigger has still to fire (first Monday 06:00 UTC); it is a non-blocking signal by design (`TESTING.md` §11.2). The `contract-fixture` required-set row still activates with `TASK-037`, which lands the first `TEST-CONTRACT-*` case.

### LOG-0077 · 2026-10-02 · B2 closes: the closure audit found three defects in its own documentation (`TASK-103`, `TASK-106` residue)

- **Event:** the question "can we assure B2 is fully done?" was answered by re-checking every `TASK-107` acceptance criterion against the repository rather than trusting the rows. Three defects surfaced, all introduced by the remediation itself: `HANDOFF.md` §8 documented `./gradlew test` as running the shared suites; §1.2 still said two repository settings were pending; and `WorkflowGateReproductionTest` cited `B2_AUTHORIZATION`, a working artifact the repository never contained.
- **Rationale:** `DEC-046` makes a document that contradicts the repository a defect, and a task row is not evidence. The first two defects are the same error in two places — `TASK-103` corrected the false command in `README.md` §9 and the same claim survived in `HANDOFF.md` — which is why the closure was verified by measurement (`--dry-run`) and not by reading.
- **Affected artifacts:** `docs/HANDOFF.md` §1.2 and §8; `build-logic/convention/src/test/kotlin/policy/WorkflowGateReproductionTest.kt` (a doc comment only); this entry. The correction landed as `876ff40` (PR #105).
- **Decision / ADR:** none — a correction inside `DEC-046`'s rule. No behaviour, dependency or contract changed.
- **Validation:** observed 2026-10-02 on merged `main` `c57ae85`. `./gradlew test --dry-run` selects **4 tasks, all Android**, with zero matches for the shared suites — the false claim; `./gradlew allTests :build-logic:convention:test --dry-run` selects **329 tasks** including `:core:testing:testAndroidHostTest`, `:core:testing:iosSimulatorArm64Test` and `:build-logic:convention:test` — the replacement. Every one of the 16 distinct commands in `HANDOFF.md` §8 resolves, except the three rows explicitly marked "Not run" for tasks that do not exist yet (`:core:ios`, the two Roborazzi tasks). The settings row matches the API. `./gradlew check`, `ktlintCheck`, `verifyRepositoryHygiene` (0 findings over 2024 objects) and the reproduction suite are green. The packet was prepared by the agent in `HANDOFF.md` §1.3 and applied as a repository setting, which stays an owner action (`DEC-049`, `AGENTS.md` §4.2).

- **Validation:** observed 2026-10-02 from the GitHub API. Before: `GET repos/davidru85/RickAndMorty/rulesets/24241444` → `enforcement: active`, rules `deletion`, `non_fast_forward`, `pull_request`, `required_status_checks` with `[android, ios]` on `integration_id 15368`, `bypass_actors: []`. After: the same read → `required_status_checks` with `[android]` only, the other three rules and the empty bypass list unchanged. On pull request #110 the run on the phase head (`13e66a45`) is `android`, and no `ios` context is reported or awaited; the rerun of that run was triggered at the owner's request (`gh run rerun 37050825465`) and concluded success (attempt 2).
- **Not verified:** that a merge is now accepted was **not** executed — merging is human-only (`DEC-049`); the enforcement state is read from the ruleset and from the pull request's check rollup, not from a completed merge. ### LOG-0134 · 2026-10-05 · Code-review remediation, phase P0 (`TASK-111`): crashes, stuck states, races and the iOS image host bypass

- **Event:** the three code-review inputs in `prompts/code_review/` were consolidated into one master list (`DEC-122`). `code_review_claude.md` carries 90 findings and a P0–P3 prescription, `code_review_codex.md` a ten-section prescription, and `code_review_glm.md` only the audit prompt, with no findings. The list is split into `TASK-111`…`TASK-115`, one pull request per phase, each starting from merged `main` (`DEC-063`). Phase P0 is implemented on `fix/review-remediation-p0` in nine increments, each with a `test:` commit observed red before its `fix:`/`feat:` commit.
- **Authorization:** the owner's instruction of 2026-10-05 to implement the reviews, read with the Claude prescription's §0.2, is recorded as the `DEC-064` task-scoped authorization for `core/**`, `feature/**`, `androidApp/**`, `iosApp/**`, `docs/**` and both READMEs. `build-logic/**`, `.github/**`, the version catalog and `VERSION` were not touched. The owner can narrow this record.
- **Every claim was reproduced before it was fixed.** The reds, in the order fixed:
  - A Detail retry raced the load it superseded (`TEST-UNIT-069`).
  - A double-tapped Delete cleared the favourites twice (`TEST-UNIT-050`).
  - `ResponseCache` turned a `CancellationException` into a miss on read, write and evict (`TEST-UNIT-068`, from the Codex prescription).
  - The rate-limit message crashed Android with `IllegalFormatConversionException: d != java.lang.String`, and Favorites never substituted its countdown (`TEST-UNIT-067`, `TEST-UI-018`, `DEC-123`).
  - Discovery's intent loop waited behind an append, refresh or retry (`TEST-UNIT-065`).
  - A failed append over content had no field to travel in, and Retry over stale content sent nothing (`TEST-UNIT-066`, `TEST-UI-019`, `DEC-124`).
  - The iOS Detail never started its shared holder (`TEST-UNIT-070`).
  - The iOS pipeline fetched portraits from any host, and admitted a `404` to its caches (`TEST-UNIT-071`, `DEC-126`).
  - The Detail's Share did nothing on either platform (`TEST-UI-020`, `DEC-125`).
- **Worse than reported:** on iOS, a rate limit with 30 s of advice rendered "Try again in 4601199584 s.". The helper looked for `%ld` while the template says `%1$ld`, so the countdown string was passed as an object pointer.
- **Decisions taken in the change, and why:**
  - `IC-017`'s arguments are typed (`MessageArgument.Number`/`Text`), so no platform guesses a type from a template.
  - `IC-018` gains `contentFailure` and `isRefreshing`. Both are breaking for the Swift initialiser, and the iOS call sites change in the same commits.
  - Android's stale strip becomes the specified snackbar with Retry, and iOS's top capsule a bottom glass banner with Retry. Their baselines (`discovery-stale`, both platforms) were re-recorded only after the new captures were compared with `UI_SPEC.md` §8. `detail-error-without-header` was re-recorded on Android for the same reason: Share is disabled there because no header exists to share.
- **Narrowed or deferred, with the reason:**
  - `TEST-UNIT-070`'s first version asserted that a stored favourite reads marked from a set built in Swift. A probe showed this cannot hold: Kotlin boxes a value class inside a collection, and the box reaches Swift as an opaque object (`CopyKeys.all` yields `CopyKey(value=…)`, and `contains("status_alive")` is `false`). The case now asserts the adapter's own duty, the load and the subscription. The reconciliation stays with `TEST-UNIT-004`.
  - Hiding the iOS system navigation bar and tab bar on Detail (review item 69) moves to `TASK-114`. Without a UI-test harness, the edge-swipe gesture cannot be verified after the bar is hidden.
- **Caught by the repository's own gate:** the first allow-list test named the live host as a literal, which `verifyNoLiveHosts` (`TEST-UNIT-024`) rejects. It now uses `RickAndMortyApi.HOST`, like the other remote tests (`3144a8c`).

  - Two Android launcher icons on a debug install: `TASK-117`, on its own branch `fix/single-launcher-entry` from `main`. The diagnostics panel lost its `LAUNCHER` filter and is reached through a debug-only app shortcut. The debug APK lists one launchable activity, and the release APK carries neither the shortcut nor the panel.

The run wrote 147 XML reports: 870 tests, 0 failures. `:androidApp:testDebugUnitTest` was up to date, and its last run on this tree reported 44 tests, 0 failures.

### LOG-0137 · 2026-10-05 · `TASK-116`: REST and GraphQL request logs in debug builds on both platforms, and a latent iOS crash

- **Event:** the owner asked for the REST and GraphQL logs and chose the app's own request events, visible in debug builds on both platforms. Two facts were already established:
  - The shared path already emitted them: `LOG-001` names the protocol, and each completion joins its start through the correlation id. `TEST-UNIT-072` pins this over the production adapters and repository, a switch included; it passed on the existing code and was not a red.
  - Android's debug variant already showed them in Logcat.
- **The iOS gap, and two defects found on the way:**
  - **iOS logged nothing below `ERROR` in any build.** The graph now binds the logger by the Kotlin binary variant, through `Platform.isDebugBinary` (`DEC-127`). `MultiverseBootstrap.logger()` gives Swift the same contract. `TEST-UNIT-073` was red in a Debug build ("a Debug build logs each request start (LOG-001)").
  - **The iOS sink crashed on every record.** `NSLog("%@ …")` received Kotlin strings, which reach a C variadic call as C strings: the app's crash report faulted at an address made of the bytes of "DEBUG". In release only `ERROR` records reach the sink, so on iOS the first failed request (offline, timeout, server) would have ended the app. `TEST-UNIT-074` was red ("Child process terminated with signal 10: Bus error"); the sink now passes one `%s` argument.
  - **The two sinks printed different lines.** Android printed enum names (`PROTOCOL=`), iOS printed the record's insertion order. `TEST-UNIT-075` was red on both halves; both now print the catalogue's wire names in its order.
- **Local tooling found, not fixed (`GAP-033`):** Xcode does not relink the app when only the static Kotlin framework changes, so the first green attempt ran the old sink and crashed; a clean build was needed. A Release simulator build also tries an `x86_64` slice the framework lacks. The Release verification below passed `ONLY_ACTIVE_ARCH=YES`.
- **Environment:** every "iPhone 17" simulator had disappeared from this Mac since the morning. One iPhone 17 on iOS 27.0 was recreated (`xcrun simctl create`), because the repository's iOS tests and baselines pin that device.
- **Verification (observed):**
  - `:core:data:testAndroidHostTest --tests '*ProtocolRequestLoggingTest*'` — 2 tests, 0 failures.
  - `:core:data:iosSimulatorArm64Test` — 188 tests, 0 failures.
  - `:androidApp:testDebugUnitTest` — 48 tests, 0 failures.
  - After `xcodebuild clean`, `xcodebuild test` on iPhone 17 / iOS 27.0 (Debug) — 122 tests, 0 failures.
  - The same `DebugLoggingTests` built in Release (`-configuration Release ENABLE_TESTABILITY=YES ONLY_ACTIVE_ARCH=YES`) — pass, so only `ERROR` is enabled there.
  - Manual, Android debug APK on the `Pixel_9_Pro` emulator: `LOG-001 protocol=REST operation=CHARACTER_LIST pathTemplate=/character page=1 …`; after choosing GraphQL in Settings, `LOG-001 … protocol=GRAPHQL …`.
  - Manual, iOS Debug app on the simulator: `DEBUG LOG-001 protocol=REST …`; with the stored protocol set to `graphql`, `DEBUG LOG-001 … protocol=GRAPHQL …`. The preference was removed afterwards.
- **Not verified:** a physical device, which the owner allowed and was not needed. An earlier manual iOS check installed a stale DerivedData build; it was discarded and repeated with the build under test.
- **Affected documents:** `docs/OBSERVABILITY.md` §2.1, `docs/DECISION_BOARD.md` (`DEC-127`), `docs/TESTING.md`, `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`GAP-033`), `docs/BACKLOG.md` (`TASK-116`), `docs/HANDOFF.md`, and this entry.

## 3. Verification performed on this repository

Verification was documentation-only for the whole lifetime of the repository up to `LOG-0025`. The first executed verification of any artifact is `LOG-0026` (2026-09-30), and the build was re-verified under the pinned daemon JDK in the same change, which built the Gradle/KMP skeleton and ran the commands it lists; before that entry, no build, test, lint, static-analysis, benchmark or application run had ever been executed here, because the repository contained no source code and no build files.

No feature code exists yet, so no test, lint, static-analysis, benchmark or application run is possible beyond the build checks recorded in `LOG-0026`, the dependency-policy checks hardened in `LOG-0033`, `LOG-0034` and `LOG-0035` (`verifyDependencyPolicy`: `TEST-UNIT-013`, `TEST-UNIT-014`, `TEST-UNIT-051`; rule set P1–P8, R1–R7, I1–I9; seed matrix S1–S63), the repository-hygiene check created in `LOG-0036` and corrected in `LOG-0037` and `LOG-0038` (`verifyRepositoryHygiene`: `TEST-UNIT-026`; path rules `HYG-01`…`HYG-07`, credential classes `SEC-026-01`…`SEC-026-12`; original matrix S1–S14 plus the review-regression matrices R1–R15 and T1–T14; `LOG-0038` is the current evidence). Nothing in this log is evidence that the described product compiles, runs or behaves as specified; the build skeleton proves the module graph and the toolchain pins, and nothing else. The documentation checks referenced above are recorded in `docs/DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` and were re-run on 2026-09-30.
- **Also removed:** a duplicated change-log row in `BACKLOG.md` (`Post-merge review corrections registered`, added during `TASK-090`) that still asserted `TASK-088`…`TASK-090` were `In review`; the original row with the `DEC-071` resolution remains.
### LOG-0097 · 2026-10-04 · The `android` gate stops paying for the Kotlin/Native it never runs
- **Event:** resolving `GAP-028` (`DEC-111`, `TASK-109`), the pull-request workflow's `android` job now runs `./gradlew check -x iosSimulatorArm64Test` and its ceiling is 60 minutes. `WorkflowGateGuard` gained `HOST_DISABLED_EXCLUSIONS` (the `android` job may exclude `iosSimulatorArm64Test` and nothing else; the `ios` job may exclude nothing), and `TEST-UNIT-060` pins that rule. Red was observed before green: with the guard unfixed, `:build-logic:convention:test --tests '*WorkflowGateGuardTest*'` reported 3 failures — `a gate excluding the suite the linux host disables is accepted`, `a gate excluding any other task is rejected`, and `the real workflows pass every rule`. Green after the rule: the same 40-test class passes, and `:build-logic:convention:check ktlintCheck` is BUILD SUCCESSFUL.
- **Rationale:** PR #145's `android` check could not go green, so the owner could not merge. The root cause is not the ceiling but the task graph: the KMP plugin *disables* `iosSimulatorArm64Test` on a non-Apple host (run `37161941905`, 2026-10-04: `w: Native task 'iosSimulatorArm64Test' is disabled` / `cannot run on the current host (linux-x86_64)`), yet `check` → `allTests` → that task carried `downloadKotlinNativeDistribution` (07:42:50 → 07:55:00 in that run; 13 m 44 s in `37142102522`, 15 m 09 s in `37156505683`) and `compileKotlinIosSimulatorArm64` for all ten KMP modules. Four consecutive runs on the B5 phase heads read `completed/cancelled` at the 45-minute ceiling; on `37161941905` the cancelled step's last recorded task was `:feature:discovery:check`. Excluding the one disabled task prunes exactly that subtree and leaves every Linux-executable check untouched. A plain raised ceiling would have masked the waste; a bare `-x` would have been a narrowed gate, so the exclusion is an allow-list keyed by job and pinned by a test.

### LOG-0099 · 2026-10-04 · Parallel CI exposes and fixes the actual policy and APK stalls

- **Event:** the first modular run [37196552961](https://github.com/davidru85/RickAndMorty/actions/runs/37196552961), head `404e9a8`, passed 15 worker checks. `repository policies` stopped at `verifyRepositoryHygiene` (10:49:45 UTC) and exceeded 20 minutes. Both APKs were assembled by 10:52:24 UTC, but the artifact worker stopped at `verifySdkLevels` (10:52:54 UTC); the already-failed run was cancelled to recover its log. The `android` result correctly failed on the cancelled workers. Logs were retrieved through GH CLI; no browser login, repository setting or suite waiver was needed.
- **Rationale and fixes:** `GitRepository.forEachBlob` wrote every request before reading responses, so Git could block on stdout while the writer blocked on stdin. It now sends and flushes one object request, drains that object's validated response, and repeats; the full object stream, byte counts and fail-closed parser are preserved. `VerifySdkLevelsTask` declared the whole SDK as an input directory, pulling unrelated installed NDKs/system images into Gradle snapshotting. It now tracks the actual APK and selected `aapt2` file; the SDK directory is only a locator. The actual SDK-level validation is preserved. Worker ceilings are reduced to 10–15 minutes, with 5 for the result.
- **Decision / scope:** implementation of the owner-requested `TASK-110` / `DEC-112` remediation within `build-logic/convention/` and the workflow; no production behaviour, dependency or architecture change. The owner's follow-up explicitly identified both long-running checks as defects.
- **Red evidence:** `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:test --tests '*GitBlobStreamTest*' --tests '*VerifySdkLevelsInputsTest*' --console=plain` ran 2 tests and failed both: real Git batch traffic with 32,768 object requests exceeded the bounded test's 15-second limit, and the task input model included the SDK tree instead of the selected tool. The timeout bounds this tooling transport regression; no product timing behaviour is tested with wall-clock time.
- **Green evidence:** `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:check verifyRepositoryHygiene :androidApp:verifySdkLevels :androidApp:verifyReleaseArtifact verifyWorkflowGate verifyDocumentedGate --console=plain` passed in 1 minute (325 actionable tasks: 311 executed, 12 from cache, 2 up-to-date). All 166 tooling tests passed; the stream regression consumed every start, byte and end in 6.261 seconds. Real hygiene scanned 1768 unique blobs with 0 findings; the real APK inspections passed, including SDK levels. Configuration-cache storage succeeded.
- **Affected artifacts:** the two convention tasks and their two regression classes, `.github/workflows/pull-request.yml`, `TESTING.md`, `DECISION_BOARD.md`, `BACKLOG.md`, `DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md`, `HANDOFF.md` and this entry.
- **Not verified yet:** GitHub execution of the corrected head. `GAP-028` stays open and `TASK-110` stays in progress until its required `android` result is observed.

### LOG-0102 · 2026-10-04 · B6 Phase 6.1: screenshot baselines, the accessibility verification, and two defects they exposed

- **Event:** `TASK-045` and `TASK-046` (Phase 6.1 of `DEC-107`) on `feat/b6-phase-6-1`. `TASK-045` stands up the Roborazzi harness (`DEC-034`) and commits the baselines for the `UI_SPEC.md` §1.1 screens and the `TESTING.md` §8.2 components, each captured in system light and dark and proved byte-identical (`TEST-UI-012`); the seven `ERROR_FLOW.md` states get rendered snapshots (`TEST-UI-016`). `TASK-046` adds the automated accessibility cases (`TEST-A11Y-002`…`006`).
- **Observed red, then green:** the first `:core:designsystem:testDebugUnitTest` run failed the `TEST-UI-012` case with "the `components` baselines must both be committed"; recording them and re-running passed. The guard regression for the new snapshot rows was observed red first — removing `:feature:settings:verifyRoborazziAndroidHostTest` from the workflow made `verifyWorkflowGate` report `TEST-UNIT-044: … is not an executable step of the `app-tests` gate job`; restoring it passed. `./gradlew :build-logic:convention:test` passed with the new case included.
- **Defect 1 — the tone-30 accent container broke `REQ-UX-003` (`DEC-114`).** While extending `TEST-A11Y-002` with the dynamic accent pairs — which commit `190a4de` claimed to add and did not: the file was never changed by it — a probe over the clamp bounds × the hue circle measured On Surface text over the emitted container at **2.79:1** (a saturated green, `#249E24`), against the 4.5:1 floor and `UI_SPEC.md` §5.4's own "keeps it at 4.5:1 or better". Root cause: `TonalPalette.fromHueChromaTone` placed the tone on the **mean sRGB channel**, so the container sat at L* 40–57 rather than the 30 the specification means — established, not assumed, from its six recorded reference accents, which all measure L* 30.0 ± 0.2. The tone is now solved in CIE L* on the same transfer function and `Y` weights `TEST-A11Y-002` uses; the six references reproduce (three exactly, three within one 8-bit step of the recorded value) and the worst case over the whole circle is **7.33:1**. `TEST-A11Y-002` now carries those pairs (48 of them), and the accent case asserts L* rather than the mean channel — the quantity the old construction fixed, which is exactly why it could not see the defect. `NOTICE` records the corrected deviation.
- **Defect 2 — the grid never collapsed (`DEC-114`).** `REQ-UX-006` / `AC-REQ-UX-006-1` require grids to drop to one column at the largest accessibility text sizes; Discovery and Favorites both hard-coded `StaggeredGridCells.Fixed(2)`. A single shared `MultiverseGrid.columns()` in `:core:designsystem` now decides the count from the configured font scale (two below 1.5, one at and above), so the two screens cannot disagree and the rule is testable as a pure function (`TEST-A11Y-005` asserts the point and both sides of it).
- **Gate activation (`DEC-071`):** the snapshot row becomes mandatory in this change. `:core:designsystem:check` depends on `verifyRoborazziDebug`, and the `app-tests` worker runs the app's and the four feature modules' verification tasks; `ModularWorkflowGate.requiredCommands` pins all five, and `TEST-UNIT-061` gained a regression that fails when any of them is dropped.
- **Not verified and stated as such:** the `DEC-023` manual checklist must be executed on a **real device** and recorded on the milestone issue. No physical device is attached to this workstation, so the checklist is **not executed**; the automated cases explicitly do not claim screen-reader behaviour, and `M1-5` remains open on that item.
- **Affected documents:** `DECISION_BOARD.md` (`DEC-114`), `core/designsystem/…/color/NOTICE`, `docs/PROJECT_LOG.md` (this entry). `TASK-045`/`TASK-046` rows, `TESTING.md` §14.2/§16 and `HANDOFF.md` follow with the phase record.

### LOG-0105 · 2026-10-04 · B7 Phase 7.1: the iOS app target, the restored `ios` gate, and the framework's real shape

- **Event:** `TASK-078`, `TASK-051` and the workflow half of `TASK-108` (Phase 7.1 of `DEC-108`) on `feat/b7-phase-7-1`.
- **`TASK-078`:** `:core:ios` exports the five `:feature:*` modules, `:core:domain` and `:core:presentation` through `api` and links `:core:data` as an unexported `implementation` (`DEC-091`). Two ADR-0012 statements were **false as written and corrected in code**: (1) the ADR says the module "carries no source file", but a Kotlin Multiplatform module with an empty source set produces **no framework at all** — the linker task is `NO-SOURCE` and no `.framework` is written, so `MultiverseBootstrap` is the module's one type and it earns its place by compile-checking the export list and naming the `:core:domain` interface the Swift graph starts around; (2) `R17` required every KMP library to declare the Android target, which ADR-0012 forbids for this module, so the rule now exempts `:core:ios` explicitly and `R12` still asserts its Apple-only set. Observed: `MultiverseExplorer.framework` links; its header carries the four state contracts, the five route declarations, `ApiFailure`, `LoadState` and `CharacterCardUi`, and carries **zero** `:core:data` types — the ADR's own "exported surface equals the per-feature surface" criterion.

Observed on a simulator build: `BUILD SUCCEEDED` with 11,997 Kotlin symbols in the app's binary, so the linkage is load-bearing rather than declared. `CFBundleShortVersionString` is `0.1.0` from the single `VERSION` source through the generated `iosApp/App/Version.xcconfig` (`tools/ios-version.sh`, `AC-REQ-NFR-006-3`, `DEC-067`); the deployment floor in the built app is `18.0` (`AC-REQ-PLAT-003-1`). The **human half remains**: `TASK-108` re-adds the `ios` required context to the `main protection` ruleset (`DEC-049`), and until the owner applies it the workflow reports `ios` while the ruleset requires only `android`.
- **Affected documents:** `CONTRIBUTING.md` §5.3 (the suspension's end), `PROJECT_LOG.md` (this entry). `TASK-078`/`TASK-051` rows and `HANDOFF.md` follow with the phase record.

### LOG-0106 · 2026-10-04 · B7 Phase 7.2: the iOS design system and the state holders

- **Event:** `TASK-052` and `TASK-053` (Phase 7.2 of `DEC-108`) on `feat/b7-phase-7-2`.
- **`TASK-052`:** `iosApp/DesignSystem` carries the token families of `docs/figma/tokens.json`, the `UI_SPEC.md` §4.2/§1.2 glass components and the iOS 18 material fallback. The parity test reads the committed export in both directions and was observed red then green: seeding `Schemes/Primary` to `#000000` failed it, restoring passed. `GlassSurface.swift` is the single place the OS path is chosen, so the fallback cannot drift from the glass path. Observed: `xcodebuild test` 14 tests 0 failures, `tools/swift-lint.sh iosApp` 0 violations over 16 files.
- **`TASK-053`:** the four iOS state holders are thin `ObservableObject` adapters over the shared reducers and holders, so no iOS surface re-derives a rule (`CONTRACTS.md` R2). Four interop facts were answered in `:core:ios` rather than worked around in Swift: Kotlin/Native exports `CoroutineScope` as a protocol with no constructor and no `cancel`, so `MultiverseBootstrap` gained `screenScope()`, `mainDispatcher()` and `cancelScope(scope:)`; the Swift-visible scope and dispatcher types are the `Kotlinx_coroutines_core*` ones; the state classes have no companion, so initial state is read with a checked cast; and Swift 6 refuses a nonisolated `deinit` that touches a non-Sendable stored property, so the observation task carries the cancellation. `.swiftlint.yml`'s `force_cast` opt-in caught the first draft's four force casts as real violations.
- **`GAP-030` registered:** the two Swift quality tools contradict each other on a multiline collection literal — `.swift-format`'s `multiElementCollectionTrailingCommas: true` requires a trailing comma and SwiftLint's `trailing_comma` forbids one. Reproduced inside the target with a probe file and recorded rather than silently worked around; the fix is a configuration decision that belongs to the Swift tooling row.
- **Affected documents:** `DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`GAP-030`), `PROJECT_LOG.md` (this entry), `BACKLOG.md` (`TASK-052`/`TASK-053` rows).

### LOG-0107 · 2026-10-04 · B7 Phase 7.3: the iOS copy set and the cross-platform parity case

- **Event:** `TASK-060` (Phase 7.3 of `DEC-108`) on `feat/b7-phase-7-3`. The first half of this phase is the iOS copy set; the shell (`TASK-054`) follows in the same pull request.
- **`TASK-060`:** `iosApp/Resources/{en,es}.lproj/Localizable.strings` carry the 58 canonical `CopyKeys`, generated from the Android copy set, which `DEC-095` makes the authored source. The `CONF-70` obligation is discharged: `CrossPlatformCopyParityTest` hands **both shipped resource folders** to the same `CopyParity.verify` the fixture mutations prove, so `TASK-013`'s deliberate `MissingResource(APPLE, …)` allowance no longer applies. Observed red then green — seeding `"action_retry" = "Volver a intentar"` in the Spanish file failed the case with a `DivergentValue`, restoring it passed. A first run of that seed appeared green because Gradle served an up-to-date result; the check is now run with `--rerun-tasks` when it is used as evidence.
- **A real generation defect was caught by the verifier itself:** the first iOS files carried a bare `%s` where the Android source does, and the verifier reported it as a `DivergentValue` in both locales (`characters_count`, `empty_search_message`). The generator now numbers an unpositioned placeholder in the order it appears, which is the value the Android formatter substitutes, and the two files agree.

The design system's `README.md` is excluded from the target, because a non-code file in the bundle breaks code signing — observed as `code object is not signed at all … In subcomponent: README.md`.

### LOG-0108 · 2026-10-04 · B7 Phase 7.3: the iOS shell

The Characters surface now names its own destination and count. The second run shows the correct Spanish copy, the four tabs in the specification's order and the single dark appearance.

### LOG-0109 · 2026-10-04 · The restored `ios` gate's three CI defects

- **Event:** restoring the `ios` job (`TASK-051`) made the gate real, and the first runs on the pushed head exposed three defects the local runs could not: the runner's Xcode is not the locked one, `.gitignore` hid a file the repository requires, and the guard's own fixtures still named the old runner.
- **The runner:** `tools/swift-tools.lock` pins swift-format to Xcode 27.0 while `macos-latest` ships Xcode 26.x, so the quality step failed closed with `swift-format is pinned to Xcode 27.0 but the selected toolchain is 26.6` — the correct behaviour for a gate that must not silently use another formatter. Xcode 27 is published on the `xcode-27` image (verified against the runner-image toolset listing on 2026-10-04), so the job moves there and **both** guard rules assert it: `WorkflowGateGuard` and `ModularWorkflowGate` now fail a job moved back to a runner without the locked toolchain.
- **The ignore rule:** `iosApp/*.xcodeproj/project.xcworkspace/` made `contents.xcworkspacedata` tracked *and* ignored; `HYG-01`/`HYG-02` reported that the file would disappear from a fresh clone. The rule is removed and the KMP template's own negation is what the repository already intended.
- **Observed:** `verifyRepositoryHygiene` was FAILED and is BUILD SUCCESSFUL after the removal; `:build-logic:convention:test` and `verifyWorkflowGate` were FAILED naming `macos-latest` and are BUILD SUCCESSFUL after the runner change.
- **Still open:** `TASK-108`'s ruleset half — the owner re-adds the `ios` required context (`DEC-049`).

### LOG-0110 · 2026-10-04 · B8 Phase 8.1: the iOS journey, and a grid defect found on the device

- **Event:** `TASK-055`, `TASK-057`, `TASK-058` (Phase 8.1 of `DEC-109`) on `feat/b8-phase-8-1`. The iOS Discovery, Detail and Favorites screens, the zoom transition's Reduce Motion fallback and the image cache.
- **Delivered and verified:** the screens render every `ERROR_FLOW.md` state from the shared `IC-018`/`IC-019`/`IC-020` contracts, with 30 state-render cases plus the favourites and cache suites. `CharacterCardCell` is one card implementation reused by both grids, so the two surfaces cannot drift. `PortraitMotion` carries the zoom/parallax/cross-fade decisions with five cases. `ImagePipelineCacheTests` asserts the two acceptance criteria: a second render issues **zero** requests (counting through the injected seam, including a cold-memory/disk-tier case) and a render writes **one** entry into the image cache and **zero** into a separate JSON cache under the same URL, with the disk layer never `URLCache.shared`. `CardNameRenderTests` renders the real cell through the glass surface, inside its `Button`, and counts the painted name pixels — added because of the defect below. Observed: `xcodebuild test` 72 tests, 0 failures; `tools/swift-lint.sh iosApp` 0 violations over 34 files.
- **`:core:ios` gained the graph facade** the Swift shell needs: Koin's `startKoin` is a Kotlin DSL and its parameterised `factory<CharacterPager>` cannot be invoked from Swift, so the composition root — `iOSGraph` plus `MultiverseBootstrap.characterPager`/`characterDetailDependencies`/`favoritesDependencies`/`settingsDependencies` — starts the same modules the Android shell loads and hands Swift concrete values. Kotlin/Native exports `CoroutineScope` as a protocol without a constructor or `cancel`, and has no `GlobalContext` object (`KoinPlatformTools.defaultContext()` is the platform-neutral accessor), so both are answered in Kotlin rather than worked around in Swift.
- **`TASK-058`'s storage half:** the response cache had **no iOS backend at all** — `FileCacheStorage` was Android-only. The entry codec and the file-name hash moved into `commonMain` so both stores read and write one format, and `NsFileCacheStorage` is the iOS peer. `CacheEntryCodecTest` pins the format's round trip and the hash against the published `FIPS 180-4` vectors.
- **`GAP-031` — the grid defect, reproduced on the device and not yet explained.** The running app's Discovery grid paints its **skeleton** cards while `characters_count` shows the server's 826. The evidence rules out the layers around it: the shared reducer reaches `LoadStateContent` with 20 items and `totalCount=826`; the holder republishes it; `DiscoveryScreen`'s body receives `LoadStateContent` with 40 items; the image pipeline resolves a live avatar URL; and the real `CharacterCardCell`, wrapped in its `Button` and rendered through the glass surface, paints visible name pixels in `CardNameRenderTests`. One speculative fix (explicit branch identities on the grid) was tried, observed not to change the rendering, and **reverted** rather than left in place. The defect is recorded as `GAP-031` (S2) rather than papered over, and `TASK-059`'s committed baselines are the check that would catch it.
- **Affected documents:** `DOCUMENTATION_AUDIT.md` (`GAP-031`), `PROJECT_LOG.md` (this entry), `BACKLOG.md` (`TASK-055`/`TASK-057`/`TASK-058` rows).

### LOG-0115 · 2026-10-04 · B9 Phase 9.2 (continued): the release-note generator
